---
name: aster-code-review
description: Review code against Asterinas's persona-keyed coding guidelines and write a Markdown review file. Use when asked to review a Git change (diff mode) or a set of target files (files mode) for defects, or inside a write-test-review loop.

# aster-code-review

Review code against Asterinas's coding guidelines
— the persona-keyed pages under `book/src/to-contribute/coding-guidelines/`
— and write one Markdown review file.
The review is **recall-first**: miss as few real defects as possible.
It makes both objective calls (undeniable bugs) and subjective calls (guideline violations, each grounded in a cited rule).

There are two review modes, both **anchored at the current checkout (HEAD)**:

- **`diff <base>`** — a Git change:
  the **commit series** the branch adds over its merge-base with `<base>`
  — each commit's message *and* its diff,
  so the review covers per-commit intent and commit hygiene, not just the net change.
  (Commit first; uncommitted edits are not reviewed in diff mode.)
- **`files <path[:lines] ...>`**
  — target code: the working-tree contents of the named files,
  optionally narrowed to line ranges.

To review at a specific commit, check it out first (it becomes HEAD).

This skill is agent-agnostic (Claude Code and Codex).
Only step 3's spawn primitive differs between agents;
everything else is identical.

## Interface

However the skill is triggered,
it receives ONE raw argument string that begins with a mode word:

```
diff   <base>              <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
files  <path[:lines] ...>  <output> [--overwrite] [--per-persona-context=auto|yes|no]
```

- `<base>` (diff) — **required**,
  any ref or SHA;
  reviews the commit series `merge-base(<base>, HEAD)..HEAD` (each commit's message + diff).
- `<path[:lines] ...>` (files) — **required**, one or more targets in the working tree.
  A target is a path, optionally `path:N-M` or `path:N-M,K-L` (1-based, inclusive);
  repeat a path to add ranges.
  Wrap a path in double quotes to allow spaces or colons (`"a: b.rs":10-20`).
- `<output>` — **required, last positional** (`cp src... dest` style);
  refuse to overwrite unless `--overwrite`.
- `--overwrite` — replace the output file if it already exists.
- `--per-persona-context` — `yes`: fan out one isolated agent per persona (best recall).
  `no`: one combined agent reviews all personas (cheaper, lower recall).
  `auto` (default) currently resolves to `yes`;
  it will become a benchmark-driven heuristic.
  Controls step 3 only.

**Do not pre-split the argument string yourself.**
Pass it verbatim, as a single quoted argument,
to `resolve_target.sh` (step 1)
— the script self-tokenizes (whitespace-split except inside double quotes),
which keeps the parse deterministic and identical across agents.

## Pipeline

Run these steps in order.
Steps 1 and 5 are deterministic scripts;
steps 6–8 use the model.

The helper scripts live in the skill's own `scripts/` directory,
at `.agents/skills/aster-code-review/scripts/` inside the repository under review.
**Invoke every script by its absolute path**
— the working directory is the code under review (the repo root), *not* the skill directory,
so a bare `scripts/…` is not found (notably on Codex, which runs commands from the repo root; do not `cd` into the skill dir either — `resolve_target.sh` needs the repo as its cwd).
Shells do not persist between commands,
so **set `SKILL` at the start of each command that uses it** (or inline the `$(git rev-parse …)`):

```sh
SKILL="$(git rev-parse --show-toplevel)/.agents/skills/aster-code-review"; "$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'
```

The steps below write `$SKILL/scripts/…` as shorthand for that absolute path.

1. **Resolve the target.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" '<raw args>'` prints the canonical review input
   — the commit series (each commit's message + diff) in `diff` mode,
   annotated file excerpts in `files` mode;
   save it to a temp file.
   Run it again with `--meta` first (`"$SKILL/scripts/resolve_target.sh" --meta '<raw args>'`) to get
   `mode=`, `base=`/`files=`, `head=`, `branch=`, `output=`, `overwrite=`, `per_persona_context=`;
   add `date=` (today) and an optional `title=` to make the meta file.
   Pass the raw argument string as a single quoted argument
   so the script's tokenizer sees it intact.
2. **Activate personas.**
   Pick which of the five personas run,
   from the reviewed paths — changed paths in `diff` mode,
   named paths in `files` mode (see *Activation*).
3. **Fan out.**
   Spawn the persona passes (see *Spawning*).
   With `per_persona_context` = `yes` or `auto` (the default),
   run **one isolated PASS per activated persona** — best recall.
   With `no`, run **one combined PASS** over all activated personas in a single context
   — cheaper, lower recall.
   Build each pass's prompt deterministically with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
   — one persona for fan-out, all activated personas for combined
   — and spawn the sub-agent with **that exact text**.
   Each pass returns a JSON array of comments;
   file each under its persona's `<fragdir>/<persona>.json` (the comment's `persona` field says which),
   so step 5 is unchanged.
4. **Collect** the per-persona JSON fragments.
5. **Assemble.**
   `"$SKILL/scripts/assemble_review.sh" [--overwrite] <meta> <fragdir> <output>` performs the deterministic merge (group by persona in fixed order, sort by file→line, drop exact duplicates *within a persona*, write frontmatter, leave a `<!-- SUMMARY -->` placeholder).
   Pass `--overwrite` only if the user gave it;
   otherwise the script refuses to clobber an existing `<output>`.
6. **Verify** (see *Verification*).
7. **Consolidate** (see *Consolidation*).
8. **Summary.**
   Replace `<!-- SUMMARY -->` with a constructive summary over the final comments:
   what the code does well, the top issues ranked by severity,
   any structural recommendations.

## Activation

A persona runs unless the reviewed paths *provably* contain nothing in its remit (reviewed paths = changed paths in `diff` mode, named paths in `files` mode):

- **maintainability, development, security** — any reviewed code.
- **hardware** — a reviewed path is assembly (`*.S`, `*.asm`),
  an architecture directory (`.../arch/...`), or contains `asm!` / `global_asm!`.
- **documentation** — a reviewed path is `book/`, any `*.md`,
  syscall-coverage files (`*.scml`), or a user-facing API surface (a syscall or kernel parameter).

Activation is path-based and deterministic;
do not use a model to triage (a wrongly-skipped persona is a silent recall hole).

## Pass contract

The shared reviewer contract
— the review methodology and the JSON comment schema every pass must follow
— lives in [`scripts/pass_contract.md`](scripts/pass_contract.md),
inlined as the **stable head** of every pass prompt by `build_pass_prompt.sh` (step 3).
Do not paraphrase it into the prompt yourself:
retyping it breaks the byte-identical prefix the prompt cache relies on (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).
A pass reads only the persona block(s) it is given (selective exposure),
reviews the **REVIEW INPUT** at the foot of the prompt,
and returns a JSON array of comments per that schema.

## Spawning a pass

Build every pass prompt with `"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input-file> <persona>...`
and spawn the sub-agent with its **exact** output
— only a script keeps the prompt's stable head (contract + guideline) byte-identical across passes and reviews,
which is what lets the prompt cache reuse it (see [`execution_model.md`](spec/execution_model.md)).

**Default (`per_persona_context` = `yes`/`auto`)**
— one pass per activated persona,
each in a CLEAN context with only its own persona block (selective exposure):

- **Claude Code** — spawn a Task sub-agent per persona.
- **Codex** — run `codex exec "<build_pass_prompt.sh output>"` per persona
  (pass the built PROMPT TEXT as the argument).

**Never spawn a pass by re-running `aster_code_review.sh` / `run_agent.sh`,
nor by re-issuing the skill's own arguments** (e.g. `codex exec … diff <base> <out>`).
A pass is a *reviewer* invocation whose input is the `build_pass_prompt.sh` text
— not another run of this skill.
Re-entering the launcher spawns another orchestrator that spawns another … , an infinite fork bomb;
the launcher now refuses it (`ACR_AGENT_RUNNING`).

Passes are independent;
order does not matter.
Collect each pass's JSON.

**Combined (`per_persona_context` = `no`)**
— build ONE prompt with all activated personas (`"$SKILL/scripts/build_pass_prompt.sh" <input> <p1> <p2> ...`)
and run a single pass;
it reviews through every lens at once and tags each comment with its `persona`.
The input and spawn overhead are paid once,
so it is cheaper
— but a larger shared context dilutes focus,
so it finds fewer defects.
The default stays fan-out:
recall comes first,
and `no` is an explicit, opt-in concession.

## Verification (step 6)

For each comment, isolate the key premise it rests on
— especially an external-system fact (Linux/POSIX behaviour, the System V ABI, Rust semantics).
Try to **refute** it:
re-read the cited code and consult an authoritative source.
Assign a verdict:

- **confirmed** — keep the comment unchanged.
- **uncertain** — keep it, but prefix `problem` with `(unverified) `.
- **refuted** — remove the comment,
  and append it to a `## Retracted by verification` list at the foot of the file with a one-line reason.

Remove **only** on confident refutation;
an unsure check is `uncertain`, not `refuted`.
This is the only step that may remove a comment, and only false positives.

## Consolidation (step 7)

Find clusters of comments that share one root cause or one fix
(e.g. several manual lock/unlock pairs that all want RAII).
For each cluster, write a single unified fix
and repoint each member's `**Fix.**` at it ("Shared with the other `raii` comments: …").
**Never remove a comment**
— every symptom stays at its own location.

## Output format

The assembled file is:
frontmatter (`date`, `mode`, `base` or `files`, `head`, `branch`, optional `title`),
a `# Summary`,
then a `## <section>` per persona in the order **Maintainability, Correctness, Security, Hardware, Documentation** (Correctness is the Development persona's section).
Each comment is a `### \`file\` line N` subsection:
a quoted ` ```diff ` snippet,
then a prose line `` `<grounding>` (<severity>): <problem> ``,
then a `**Fix.**` paragraph.
Do not restructure what the script emits;
only fill the summary and apply the verify/consolidate edits.

# Pass contract

You are a reviewer applying the persona guideline(s) included below to the change or files under review
(the **REVIEW INPUT** at the very end of this prompt).
Find as many real defects as possible within the included persona(s)' remit
— runtime correctness for Development,
security/soundness for Security,
ABI/hardware for Hardware,
doc style/currency for Documentation,
and structure/process for Maintainability
— without inventing issues; a false alarm is a real cost.

For each persona block below,
work that persona's concerns in the order its file gives.
For each candidate rule,
read its one-line gist first
and drill into the full rule (its linked subsections) only on a suspected violation.
Stay within the remit of the persona(s) you are given.

Each persona searches only for defects whose failure belongs to that persona.
Do not run a general bug sweep from every persona.
When another persona is the clear natural owner,
do not duplicate that investigation here.
For example,
Maintainability should inspect design shape, readability, naming, layout,
and commit hygiene;
it should not trace runtime permission semantics, Linux/POSIX behavior,
wrong predicates, or data-flow edge cases unless they are evidence of a
maintainability rule violation.

Within each included persona's owned failure modes,
reason about the code even when no explicit guideline names the issue.
Examples include off-by-one and reachable panic for Development,
input-validation or permission-boundary flaws for Security,
ABI/alignment hazards for Hardware,
navigation or currency defects for Documentation,
and structural or process defects for Maintainability.
Ground each non-guideline finding in a short plain-language description of the defect
("Off by one", "Use after free", "Reachable panic", …)
— not the bare word `bug`, and not a coined hyphenated short-name,
which would read as a guideline.
Never stay silent about a real defect that belongs to the included persona
because "no guideline covers it".

Be **adversarial**:
before dismissing a suspected in-scope defect as safe,
state the concrete input or interleaving that would trigger it.
Report an in-scope defect unless you can show that case cannot happen.
"It looks fine" is not a verdict.

When reviewing code that implements, wraps, emulates, or depends on an externally
specified API, ABI, language rule, or hardware contract,
consult the authoritative source needed to understand that contract before
deciding whether the code is correct.
For Linux/POSIX/kernel-facing contracts,
use the configured MCP servers as the primary lookup path instead of manually
navigating web pages from memory:

- `man_pages` for syscall-facing userspace behavior, libc/POSIX-adjacent APIs,
  sections, and documented errno behavior
  (`search_man_pages`, `get_man_page`, `list_man_sections`).
- `linux_kernel` for current Linux kernel documentation, upstream release
  information, upstream source snippets, and optional local Linux-tree searches
  (`latest_kernel_releases`, `search_kernel_docs`, `fetch_kernel_doc`,
  `search_online_kernel_code`, `fetch_upstream_kernel_file`,
  `search_local_kernel_code`, `read_local_kernel_file`,
  `search_local_kernel_api`, `inspect_kernel_capabilities`,
  `find_driver_examples`).
- `elixir_bootlin` for Bootlin Elixir source browsing, version/project listing,
  raw source reads, identifier lookup, and autocomplete
  (`list_projects`, `list_versions`, `get_raw_source`, `ident_lookup`,
  `autocomplete`).

Pick the MCP that matches the question first:
use `man_pages` for syscall/user-visible semantics,
`linux_kernel` for kernel documentation and upstream source,
and `elixir_bootlin` when Bootlin-style versioned source or identifier lookup is
the clearest way to confirm the implementation detail.
When Linux man pages do not fully specify the behavior,
navigate the version-matched Linux `Documentation/` tree as a subsystem map
through `linux_kernel` before jumping into implementation source:
identify the owning subsystem directory,
read its index/overview and focused `.rst` files for the domain model,
terminology, invariants, and source entry points,
then verify the concrete rule in matching source through `linux_kernel` or
`elixir_bootlin`.
For VFS/pathname behavior,
use `Documentation/filesystems/` as the owning subsystem documentation;
pathname lookup, `*at()` path resolution, empty pathnames, trailing slashes,
final-component handling, dentry parent semantics, mount/namei behavior,
and create/delete/link/rename permission checks should be checked against
focused filesystem documentation such as `path-lookup.rst` via `linux_kernel`
and then version-matched implementation source such as `fs/namei.c` via
`linux_kernel` or `elixir_bootlin`.
For portable userspace semantics,
start with the installed `man_pages` material and mark any POSIX premise
uncertain if the needed rule is not present there.
Use hardware vendor manuals for architecture rules,
and use the Rust Reference or official Rust standard-library documentation for Rust
language/API semantics.
Identify the relevant semantic rule, invariant, and error behavior,
then review the code against that rule;
do not rely on memory, local comments, or the implementation's apparent intent
alone.
If the source cannot be checked,
keep the claim narrow and mark the premise as uncertain rather than presenting it
as established fact.

The REVIEW INPUT is the unit of review;
you MAY read surrounding code in the working tree for extra context.

## Output

Output **only** a JSON array of comment objects (no prose around it):

```json
[{"file":"path/relative/to/repo.rs","line":42,"persona":"development","grounding":"lock-ordering","severity":"major",
  "problem":"`foo()` takes `b.lock()` while already holding `a.lock()`, the reverse of the `a`-before-`b` order elsewhere — a deadlock",
  "fix":"take `a.lock()` before `b.lock()` here too, matching the rest of the module",
  "diff":"the few relevant lines (a diff hunk, or source lines in files mode)"}]
```

- `persona` — which persona section the comment belongs to (`maintainability`, `development`, `security`, `hardware`, `documentation`);
  used to file the comment under the right section.
  In a single-persona (fan-out) pass it is always that persona.
- `grounding` — what the comment rests on, in one of two forms kept visually distinct:
  when you **cite a guideline**, its short-name
  — a lowercase kebab identifier (e.g. `lock-ordering`), rendered as code;
  when you report a **bug no guideline covers**, a short plain-language description of the defect
  (e.g. "Off by one", "Use after free", "Incorrect cleanup"), rendered as prose.
  Do not coin a hyphenated pseudo-short-name for a bug
  — that reads as a guideline
  — and never use the bare word `bug`,
  which says nothing the reader cannot already see.
- `severity` — **required**,
  one of `critical` (must fix) / `major` (should fix) / `minor` (worth fixing) / `nit` (optional or stylistic).
- `problem` and `fix` are **both required**
  — every comment proposes a remedy.
  They are posted as GitHub-flavored Markdown,
  so wrap every code identifier, path, type, function or variable name, and literal value in backticks
  (`self.len`, `Ordering::Acquire`, `kernel/src/foo.rs`),
  and put any multi-line snippet in `fix` in a fenced ```` ``` ```` block.
  (The `grounding` of a bug stays plain prose, as described above
  — only `problem` and `fix` take inline code.)
- Every field above is required on every comment,
  with a single exception for `line`, described next.
- `line` is the line the comment anchors to
  — the post-change line in the commit's diff (`diff` mode),
  or the file's line number (`files` mode).
  It is **required for a finding about code**.
- For a finding about a **commit message** (`diff` mode shows each commit's message),
  set `file` to the commit locus (e.g. `commit abc1234 message`),
  **omit `line`** (its one exception),
  and ground it in a commit-hygiene rule (`imperative-subject`, `atomic-commits`, …).
- Report only issues within the included persona(s)' remit.
  If you find nothing, output `[]`.

#!/usr/bin/env bash

# SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

#
# run_agent.sh — launch the ACR_AGENT_PROFILE agent with one prompt, headless,
# with NO shell (so a prompt full of backticks/quotes/newlines is safe).
#
# The shared launcher: aster_code_review.sh runs the skill through it,
# and the benchmark (run.sh) uses it for its grader calls.
# It is the ONE place that knows how to turn a profile into a running agent.
#
# Usage: run_agent.sh "<prompt>"
# Env:
#   ACR_AGENT_PROFILE    REQUIRED. a profile NAME -> agent_profiles/<name>/, or a dir path.
#   ACR_PROFILE_VARIANT  `smoke` merges the `.smoke` overlay over the base profile; unset = base.
#
# A profile dir holds profile.json (command/env/inherit)
# and, by convention, an optional config.toml seeded into a private {workdir}.
# {prompt}/{workdir}/{home} in the profile are substituted.
# Runs in the current cwd and inherits the current env PLUS the profile env.
set -uo pipefail
HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
SKILL="$(cd "$HERE/.." && pwd)"
profiles_dir="$SKILL/agent_profiles"

[[ $# -eq 1 ]] || { echo "usage: run_agent.sh \"<prompt>\"" >&2; exit 2; }
prompt="$1"

# Recursion guard.
# This launcher spawns the review agent,
# so being re-entered from *inside* a running agent
# means the agent mistakenly re-ran the skill launcher (aster_code_review.sh)
# instead of spawning a persona pass
# — an infinite agent fork bomb
# (observed as many nested `codex exec "Use the aster-code-review skill ..."`
# processes until the runner is killed).
# Fail fast.
# A legitimate persona pass is a plain `codex exec`/Task with a build_pass_prompt.sh prompt
# and never re-enters here;
# the env var is exported so it reaches the agent and any shell it runs.
if [[ -n "${ACR_AGENT_RUNNING:-}" ]]; then
    echo "run_agent.sh: refusing to re-enter the skill launcher from within a running agent (recursion). Spawn a persona pass as a plain 'codex exec'/Task with the build_pass_prompt.sh prompt, not aster_code_review.sh / run_agent.sh." >&2
    exit 3
fi
export ACR_AGENT_RUNNING=1

list_profiles() { find "$profiles_dir" -mindepth 2 -maxdepth 2 -name profile.json -printf '%h\n' 2>/dev/null | xargs -r -n1 basename | sort | tr '\n' ' '; }
[[ -n "${ACR_AGENT_PROFILE:-}" ]] || {
    echo "run_agent.sh: ACR_AGENT_PROFILE is required (e.g. ACR_AGENT_PROFILE=codex). Available: $(list_profiles)" >&2; exit 2; }
if [[ "$ACR_AGENT_PROFILE" == */* ]]; then PROFILE_DIR="$ACR_AGENT_PROFILE"; else PROFILE_DIR="$profiles_dir/$ACR_AGENT_PROFILE"; fi
[[ -f "$PROFILE_DIR/profile.json" ]] || { echo "run_agent.sh: profile not found: $PROFILE_DIR/profile.json (available: $(list_profiles))" >&2; exit 2; }
PROFILE_DIR="$(cd "$PROFILE_DIR" && pwd)"
PROFILE_SMOKE=0; [[ "${ACR_PROFILE_VARIANT:-}" == smoke ]] && PROFILE_SMOKE=1
PROFILE_WORKDIR="$(mktemp -d)"          # the {workdir}: config.toml + auth land here (e.g. CODEX_HOME)
trap 'rm -rf "$PROFILE_WORKDIR"' EXIT
declare -a PROFILE_CMD=() PROFILE_ENV=() INH_SRC=() INH_DEST=()

# Parse the (smoke-merged) profile.json into C<TAB>token | E<TAB>KEY=VAL | I<TAB>src<TAB>dest,
# and seed the (smoke-merged) config.toml into {workdir}.
# {workdir}/{home} are resolved now (static);
# {prompt} is left for the launch below.
profile_parsed="$(python3 - "$PROFILE_DIR" "$PROFILE_WORKDIR" "$HOME" "$PROFILE_SMOKE" <<'PY'
import json, os, sys
pdir, workdir, home, smoke = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3], sys.argv[4] == "1"
def load_json(p):
    if not os.path.exists(p): return {}
    try: return json.load(open(p))
    except Exception as e: sys.stderr.write(f"invalid JSON {p}: {e}\n"); sys.exit(3)
prof = load_json(os.path.join(pdir, "profile.json"))
if smoke: prof.update(load_json(os.path.join(pdir, "profile.smoke.json")))   # shallow: a smoke key wins
cmd = prof.get("command")
if not isinstance(cmd, list) or not cmd or not all(isinstance(x, str) for x in cmd):
    sys.stderr.write("profile 'command' must be a non-empty array of strings\n"); sys.exit(3)
def sub(s): return str(s).replace("{workdir}", workdir).replace("{home}", home)
for t in cmd:                                          print("C\t" + sub(t))
for k, v in (prof.get("env") or {}).items():           print("E\t" + f"{k}={sub(v)}")
for src, dest in (prof.get("inherit") or {}).items():  print("I\t" + sub(str(src)) + "\t" + sub(dest))
# config convention: seed config.toml into {workdir} if present, shallow-merging the
# smoke overlay. Top-level `key = value` scalars merge per-key (a smoke key wins);
# any `[table]` section (e.g. a `[model_providers.*]` for API-key auth) is preserved
# verbatim after the scalars — the flat merge cannot represent it. Comments are dropped
# from the seeded copy (the source keeps them); tables must follow the scalars, as ours do.
def toml_flat(p):        # top-level scalar keys only — stop at the first table header
    d = {}
    if os.path.exists(p):
        for ln in open(p):
            s = ln.strip()
            if s.startswith("["): break
            if s and not s.startswith("#") and "=" in s:
                k, v = s.split("=", 1); d[k.strip()] = v.strip()
    return d
def toml_tables(p):      # raw lines from the first table header to EOF, kept verbatim
    out, started = [], False
    if os.path.exists(p):
        for ln in open(p):
            if not started and ln.lstrip().startswith("["): started = True
            if started: out.append(ln.rstrip("\n"))
    return out
base = os.path.join(pdir, "config.toml")
if os.path.exists(base):
    cfg, tables = toml_flat(base), toml_tables(base)
    if smoke:
        smk = os.path.join(pdir, "config.smoke.toml")
        cfg.update(toml_flat(smk))
        st = toml_tables(smk)
        if st: tables = st                     # a smoke overlay with tables replaces (none today)
    with open(os.path.join(workdir, "config.toml"), "w") as f:
        for k, v in cfg.items(): f.write(f"{k} = {v}\n")
        if tables: f.write("\n" + "\n".join(tables) + "\n")
PY
)" || { echo "run_agent.sh: invalid profile: $PROFILE_DIR" >&2; exit 2; }
while IFS=$'\t' read -r tag a b; do
    case "$tag" in
        C) PROFILE_CMD+=("$a") ;;
        E) PROFILE_ENV+=("$a") ;;
        I) INH_SRC+=("$a"); INH_DEST+=("$b") ;;
    esac
done <<<"$profile_parsed"
for i in "${!INH_SRC[@]}"; do                     # inherit outside files (e.g. the agent's real auth)
    src="${INH_SRC[$i]}"; dest="$PROFILE_WORKDIR/${INH_DEST[$i]}"
    [[ -f "$src" ]] || { echo "run_agent.sh: profile 'inherit' source not found: $src (is the agent logged in?)" >&2; exit 2; }
    mkdir -p "$(dirname "$dest")"; cp "$src" "$dest"
done

declare -a argv=()
for tok in "${PROFILE_CMD[@]}"; do argv+=("${tok//\{prompt\}/$prompt}"); done
# stdin from /dev/null: the prompt is an argv token,
# and a headless agent that also reads stdin
# (e.g. `codex exec` appends piped stdin as a <stdin> block)
# must NOT swallow the caller's stdin (the benchmark loop feeds the problem list on another FD).
if [[ ${#PROFILE_ENV[@]} -gt 0 ]]; then env "${PROFILE_ENV[@]}" "${argv[@]}" </dev/null; else "${argv[@]}" </dev/null; fi

exec

#!/usr/bin/env bash

# SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0

#
# assemble_review.sh — deterministic merge of persona comment fragments into the review file body.
# No LLM judgement here:
# group by persona in a fixed order,
# sort by file then line within each group,
# drop exact duplicates *within a persona*,
# write the frontmatter,
# and leave a <!-- SUMMARY --> placeholder for the orchestrator's LLM summary step.
#
# Usage: assemble_review.sh [--overwrite] <meta-file> <frag-dir> <output-file>
#   meta-file: lines `mode=`, `base=` (diff) or `files=` (files), `head=`,
#              `branch=`, `date=`, `title=`
#   frag-dir:  one <persona>.json per persona, each a JSON array of comments:
#       {"file","line","grounding","severity","problem","fix","diff"}
#   --overwrite: replace <output-file> if it already exists (otherwise fail).
set -euo pipefail

overwrite=0
pos=()
for a in "$@"; do
    case "$a" in
        --overwrite) overwrite=1 ;;
        --*) echo "assemble_review.sh: unknown flag: $a" >&2; exit 2 ;;
        *) pos+=("$a") ;;
    esac
done
if [[ ${#pos[@]} -ne 3 ]]; then
    echo "usage: assemble_review.sh [--overwrite] <meta-file> <frag-dir> <output-file>" >&2
    exit 2
fi
meta="${pos[0]}"; fragdir="${pos[1]}"; out="${pos[2]}"

# Refuse to clobber an existing review unless --overwrite (design §3).
if [[ -e "$out" && $overwrite -eq 0 ]]; then
    echo "assemble_review.sh: refusing to overwrite existing $out (pass --overwrite)" >&2
    exit 1
fi

python3 - "$meta" "$fragdir" "$out" <<'PY'
import json, os, re, sys
meta_path, fragdir, out = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2], sys.argv[3]

# A guideline short-name is a lowercase kebab identifier (e.g. `lock-ordering`) and
# renders as code — a citable rule. A bug's grounding is a short plain-language
# description (e.g. "Off by one") and renders as prose, so it never reads as a rule name.
KEBAB = re.compile(r"[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*")
def grounding_tag(g):
    return "`%s`" % g if KEBAB.fullmatch(g) else g

meta = {}
for line in open(meta_path):
    line = line.strip()
    if "=" in line:
        k, v = line.split("=", 1)
        meta[k] = v

# Fixed persona order -> review section title.
# "Correctness" is the Development persona's section (a bare "## Development" reads oddly; see the design doc).
ORDER = [("maintainability", "Maintainability"),
         ("development",      "Correctness"),
         ("security",         "Security"),
         ("hardware",         "Hardware"),
         ("documentation",    "Documentation")]

def load(persona):
    p = os.path.join(fragdir, persona + ".json")
    if not os.path.exists(p):
        return []          # persona not activated / did not run: legitimately absent
    # An EXISTING fragment that is unparseable or not a JSON array means a pass
    # produced broken output. Failing closed (rather than dropping it) is essential:
    # this skill is recall-first, so a silently-discarded fragment is a silent
    # recall hole the benchmark/orchestrator would never notice.
    try:
        data = json.load(open(p))
    except Exception as e:
        sys.stderr.write(f"assemble: FATAL: unparseable fragment {p}: {e}\n")
        sys.exit(2)
    if not isinstance(data, list):
        sys.stderr.write(f"assemble: FATAL: fragment {p} is not a JSON array of comments\n")
        sys.exit(2)
    return data

L = ["---",
     f"date: {meta.get('date','')}",
     f"mode: {meta.get('mode','diff')}"]
if meta.get("base"):
    L.append(f"base: {meta['base']}")
if meta.get("files"):
    L.append(f"files: {meta['files']}")
L += [f"head: {meta.get('head','')}",
      f"branch: {meta.get('branch','')}"]
if meta.get("title"):
    # json.dumps yields a valid YAML double-quoted scalar (escapes embedded quotes).
    L.append("title: " + json.dumps(meta["title"]))
L += ["---", "", "# Summary", "", "<!-- SUMMARY -->", ""]

counts = {}
for persona, title in ORDER:
    # Dedup is per-persona: identical comments from two different personas are both kept,
    # in their own sections (design §4).
    seen = set()
    uniq = []
    for c in load(persona):
        # "Exact" duplicate: identical in EVERY field (a normalized full-object key),
        # so two comments that differ in grounding, severity, or diff are both kept.
        # A partial key would collapse distinct findings — a recall hole.
        key = json.dumps(c, sort_keys=True)
        if key in seen:
            continue
        seen.add(key)
        uniq.append(c)
    counts[persona] = len(uniq)
    if not uniq:
        continue
    # Sort by file, then line, then a stable text key
    # so co-located comments have a deterministic order regardless of pass output order.
    uniq.sort(key=lambda c: (str(c.get("file", "")),
                             int(c.get("line", 0) or 0),
                             str(c.get("grounding", "")),
                             str(c.get("problem", ""))))
    L += [f"## {title}", ""]
    for c in uniq:
        loc = "`%s`" % c.get("file", "?")
        if c.get("line"):
            loc += " line %s" % c["line"]
        L += [f"### {loc}", ""]
        diff = c.get("diff")
        if diff:
            L.append("> ```diff")
            L += ["> " + dl for dl in str(diff).splitlines()]
            L += ["> ```", ""]
        L.append("%s (%s): %s" % (grounding_tag(c.get("grounding", "issue")),
                                  c.get("severity", "major"),
                                  (c.get("problem", "") or "").strip()))
        L += ["", "**Fix.** %s" % (c.get("fix", "") or "").strip(), ""]

open(out, "w").write("\n".join(L).rstrip() + "\n")
sys.stderr.write("assemble: " + ", ".join(f"{p}={counts[p]}" for p, _ in ORDER) + "\n")
PY

# Codex config for the GitHub PR-review workflow
# (see .github/workflows/invoke_aster_code_review.yml).
# Auth is the OPENAI_API_KEY secret, inherited from the workflow env
# — so, unlike the `codex` profile, there is no `inherit` of a ChatGPT `auth.json`.
#
# API-KEY AUTH.
# codex's built-in `openai` provider authenticates as a ChatGPT account
# (a websocket transport to /v1/responses) and never attaches the API key,
# so with only OPENAI_API_KEY it fails with 401 "missing bearer".
# We therefore define an explicit provider that authenticates with the key:
# `env_key` names the env var sent as `Authorization: Bearer`,
# and `wire_api = "responses"`
# (codex v0.142 removed "chat", leaving "responses" as the only wire API)
# routes over HTTPS /v1/responses.
# `model_provider` selects it.
#
# `model` is PINNED for reproducibility;
# gpt-5.5 is served by the API key.
model = "gpt-5.5"
model_reasoning_effort = "high"
model_provider = "openai_apikey"

# SANDBOX.
# Unlike the `codex` profile (workspace-write, for a dev machine),
# the CI job runs inside an ephemeral GitHub Actions container
# that is ALREADY the isolation boundary
# — and codex's own `workspace-write` sandbox cannot run there:
# it shells out via bubblewrap,
# which needs unprivileged user namespaces that the container disables
# ("bwrap: ... kernel does not allow non-privileged user namespaces"),
# so every command codex runs is rejected.
# We therefore disable codex's in-container sandbox and let the container confine it.
# `approval_policy` stays `never` (no human in CI).
# Network is fully available (needed for the API and for docs/spec lookups during verification).
sandbox_mode = "danger-full-access"
approval_policy = "never"

# The API-key provider:
# same OpenAI endpoint, but authenticated with the key rather than a ChatGPT session.
# run_agent.sh seeds this table verbatim into CODEX_HOME.
[model_providers.openai_apikey]
name = "OpenAI (API key)"
base_url = "https://api.openai.com/v1"
wire_api = "responses"
env_key = "OPENAI_API_KEY"

 succeeded in 0ms:
# For Development

*Does the code do the right thing — including on error, concurrent, and hot paths — and is it proven by tests?*

This is the index of the **development** guidelines.
Each subsection is its own page,
and each entry below links a stable `short-name` to its guideline,
with a one-line gist so a reader (or a review tool) can grasp the guideline before opening it.

## Index

**[Correctness](correctness.md)**
- [`checked-arithmetic`](correctness.md#checked-arithmetic): Use checked or saturating arithmetic where overflow is possible; don't rely on wrapping.
- [`debug-assert`](correctness.md#debug-assert): Invariants that should never fail in correct code go in `debug_assert!`, not `assert!`.
- [`propagate-errors`](correctness.md#propagate-errors): Propagate errors with `?`; don't `.unwrap()` where failure is legitimate.

**[Concurrency](concurrency.md)**
- [`lock-ordering`](concurrency.md#lock-ordering): Establish and document one global, hierarchical lock order to avoid deadlock.
- [`no-io-under-spinlock`](concurrency.md#no-io-under-spinlock): Never do I/O or blocking work under a spinlock; drop it first or use a sleeping mutex.
- [`careful-atomics`](concurrency.md#careful-atomics): Use a lock when fields change in concert; atomics only for a genuinely independent value.
- [`atomic-critical-sections`](concurrency.md#atomic-critical-sections): Keep a check-then-act sequence under one lock acquisition (else a TOCTOU race).

**[Resource Management](resource-management.md)**
- [`raii`](resource-management.md#raii): Acquire and release every resource via `Drop`, not manual enable/disable pairs.

**[Efficiency](efficiency.md)**
- [`no-linear-hot-paths`](efficiency.md#no-linear-hot-paths): Keep hot paths (syscall dispatch, scheduler enqueue) sub-linear; reject O(n) over large n.
- [`minimize-copies`](efficiency.md#minimize-copies): Avoid needless copies/allocations — cloning an `Arc` where a reference suffices, etc.
- [`no-premature-optimization`](efficiency.md#no-premature-optimization): Justify optimizations with measured data; don't add complexity for a non-problem.

**[Observability](observability.md)**
- [`ostd-log-only`](observability.md#ostd-log-only): Use the `ostd::log` macros in OSTD-based crates, not the `log` crate directly.
- [`log-levels`](observability.md#log-levels): Match the eight severity levels to meaning — `error!` for recoverable failures, `emerg!` before halt.
- [`log-prefix`](observability.md#log-prefix): Define a crate `__log_prefix` at the crate root before any `mod`.

**[Testing](testing.md)**
- [`add-regression-tests`](testing.md#add-regression-tests): Every bug fix ships a test that would have caught it, referencing the issue.
- [`test-visible-behavior`](testing.md#test-visible-behavior): Test observable behavior through public APIs; name tests after behavior, not internals.
- [`use-assertions`](testing.md#use-assertions): Use assertion macros (clear failure messages), not print-and-eyeball.
- [`test-cleanup`](testing.md#test-cleanup): Clean up after every test (fds, temp files, child processes) to avoid flakiness.

No **path-specific** guidelines yet.

  -c, --config <key=value>
          Override a configuration value that would otherwise be loaded from `~/.codex/config.toml`.
          Use a dotted path (`foo.bar.baz`) to override nested values. The `value` portion is parsed
          as TOML. If it fails to parse as TOML, the raw string is used as a literal.
          
          Examples: - `-c model="o3"` - `-c 'sandbox_permissions=["disk-full-read-access"]'` - `-c
          shell_environment_policy.inherit=all`

      --enable <FEATURE>
          Enable a feature (repeatable). Equivalent to `-c features.<name>=true`

      --disable <FEATURE>
          Disable a feature (repeatable). Equivalent to `-c features.<name>=false`

      --strict-config
          Error out when config.toml contains fields that are not recognized by this version of
          Codex

  -i, --image <FILE>...
          Optional image(s) to attach to the initial prompt

  -m, --model <MODEL>
          Model the agent should use

      --oss
          Use open-source provider

      --local-provider <OSS_PROVIDER>
          Specify which local provider to use (lmstudio or ollama). If not specified with --oss,
          will use config default or show selection

  -p, --profile <CONFIG_PROFILE_V2>
          Layer $CODEX_HOME/<name>.config.toml on top of the base user config

  -s, --sandbox <SANDBOX_MODE>
          Select the sandbox policy to use when executing model-generated shell commands
          
          [possible values: read-only, workspace-write, danger-full-access]

      --dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox
          Skip all confirmation prompts and execute commands without sandboxing. EXTREMELY
          DANGEROUS. Intended solely for running in environments that are externally sandboxed

      --dangerously-bypass-hook-trust
          Run enabled hooks without requiring persisted hook trust for this invocation. DANGEROUS.
          Intended only for automation that already vets hook sources

  -C, --cd <DIR>
          Tell the agent to use the specified directory as its working root

      --add-dir <DIR>
          Additional directories that should be writable alongside the primary workspace

      --skip-git-repo-check
          Allow running Codex outside a Git repository

      --ephemeral
          Run without persisting session files to disk

      --ignore-user-config
          Do not load `$CODEX_HOME/config.toml`; auth still uses `CODEX_HOME`

      --ignore-rules
          Do not load user or project execpolicy `.rules` files

      --output-schema <FILE>
          Path to a JSON Schema file describing the model's final response shape

      --color <COLOR>
          Specifies color settings for use in the output
          
          [default: auto]
          [possible values: always, never, auto]

      --json
          Print events to stdout as JSONL

  -o, --output-last-message <FILE>
          Specifies file where the last message from the agent should be written

  -h, --help
          Print help (see a summary with '-h')

  -V, --version
          Print version

    28	/// A `Path` is used to represent an exact location in the VFS tree.
    29	///
    30	/// Each `Path` corresponds to a node in the VFS tree, and a single node
    31	/// may have multiple `Path` instances referencing it due to mount operations.
    32	#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    33	pub struct Path {
    34	    mount: Arc<Mount>,
    35	    dentry: Arc<Dentry>,
    36	}
    37	
    38	impl Path {
    39	    /// Creates a new `Path` to represent the root directory of a file system.
    40	    pub fn new_fs_root(mount: Arc<Mount>) -> Self {
    41	        let inner = mount.root_dentry().clone();
    42	        Self::new(mount, inner)
    43	    }
    44	
    45	    /// Creates a new `Path` to represent the child directory of a file system.
    46	    pub fn new_fs_child(&self, name: &str, type_: InodeType, mode: InodeMode) -> Result<Self> {
    47	        if self
    48	            .inode()
    49	            .check_permission(Permission::MAY_WRITE)
    50	            .is_err()
    51	        {
    52	            return_errno!(Errno::EACCES);
    53	        }
    54	        let new_child_dentry = self.dentry.create(name, type_, mode)?;
    55	        Ok(Self::new(self.mount.clone(), new_child_dentry))
    56	    }
    57	
    58	    fn new(mount: Arc<Mount>, dentry: Arc<Dentry>) -> Self {
    59	        Self { mount, dentry }
    60	    }
    61	
    62	    /// Gets the mount node of current `Path`.
    63	    pub fn mount_node(&self) -> &Arc<Mount> {
    64	        &self.mount
    65	    }
    66	
    67	    /// Returns true if the current `Path` is the root of its mount.
    68	    pub(super) fn is_mount_root(&self) -> bool {
    69	        Arc::ptr_eq(&self.dentry, self.mount.root_dentry())
    70	    }
    71	
    72	    /// Lookups the target `Path` given the `name`.
    73	    pub fn lookup(&self, name: &str) -> Result<Self> {
    74	        if self.type_() != InodeType::Dir {
    75	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "the path is not a directory");
    76	        }
    77	        if self.inode().check_permission(Permission::MAY_EXEC).is_err() {
    78	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EACCES, "the path cannot be looked up");
    79	        }
    80	        if name.len() > NAME_MAX {
    81	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENAMETOOLONG, "the path name is too long");
    82	        }
    83	
    84	        let target_path = if is_dot(name) {
    85	            self.this()
    86	        } else if is_dotdot(name) {
    87	            self.effective_parent().unwrap_or_else(|| self.this())
    88	        } else {
    89	            let target_inner_opt = self.dentry.lookup_via_cache(name)?;
    90	            match target_inner_opt {
    91	                Some(target_inner) => Self::new(self.mount.clone(), target_inner),
    92	                None => {
    93	                    let target_inner = self.dentry.lookup_via_fs(name)?;
    94	                    Self::new(self.mount.clone(), target_inner)
    95	                }
    96	            }
    97	        };
    98	
    99	        Ok(target_path.get_top_path())
   100	    }
   101	
   102	    /// Gets the absolute path.
   103	    ///
   104	    /// It will resolve the mountpoint automatically.
   105	    pub fn abs_path(&self) -> String {
   106	        let mut path_name = self.effective_name();
   107	        let mut current_dir = self.this();
   108	
   109	        while let Some(parent_dir) = current_dir.effective_parent() {
   110	            path_name = {
   111	                let parent_name = parent_dir.effective_name();
   112	                if parent_name != "/" {
   113	                    parent_name + "/" + &path_name
   114	                } else {
   115	                    parent_name + &path_name
   116	                }
   117	            };
   118	            current_dir = parent_dir;
   119	        }
   120	
   121	        debug_assert!(path_name.starts_with('/'));
   122	        path_name
   123	    }
   124	
   125	    /// Gets the effective name of the `Path`.
   126	    ///
   127	    /// If it is the root of a mount, it will go up to the mountpoint
   128	    /// to get the name of the mountpoint recursively.
   129	    fn effective_name(&self) -> String {
   130	        if !self.is_mount_root() {
   320	        }
   321	
   322	        self.mount.graft_mount_tree(dst_path)
   323	    }
   324	
   325	    /// Sets the propagation type of the mount of this `Path`.
   326	    pub fn set_mount_propagation(
   327	        &self,
   328	        prop: MountPropType,
   329	        recursive: bool,
   330	        ctx: &Context,
   331	    ) -> Result<()> {
   332	        if !self.is_mount_root() {
   333	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the path is not a mount root");
   334	        };
   335	
   336	        let current_ns_proxy = ctx.thread_local.borrow_ns_proxy();
   337	        let current_mnt_ns = current_ns_proxy.unwrap().mnt_ns();
   338	        if !current_mnt_ns.owns(&self.mount) {
   339	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the path is not in this mount namespace");
   340	        }
   341	
   342	        self.mount.set_propagation(prop, recursive);
   343	
   344	        Ok(())
   345	    }
   346	
   347	    /// Creates a `Path` by making an inode of the `type_` with the `mode`.
   348	    pub fn mknod(&self, name: &str, mode: InodeMode, type_: MknodType) -> Result<Self> {
   349	        let inner = self.dentry.mknod(name, mode, type_)?;
   350	        Ok(Self::new(self.mount.clone(), inner))
   351	    }
   352	
   353	    /// Links a new name for the `Path`.
   354	    pub fn link(&self, old: &Self, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
   355	        if !Arc::ptr_eq(&old.mount, &self.mount) {
   356	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EXDEV, "the operation cannot cross mounts");
   357	        }
   358	
   359	        self.dentry.link(&old.dentry, name)
   360	    }
   361	
   362	    /// Renames a `Path` to the new `Path` by `rename()` the inner inode.
   363	    pub fn rename(&self, old_name: &str, new_dir: &Self, new_name: &str) -> Result<()> {
   364	        if !Arc::ptr_eq(&self.mount, &new_dir.mount) {
   365	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EXDEV, "the operation cannot cross mounts");
   366	        }
   367	
   368	        self.dentry.rename(old_name, &new_dir.dentry, new_name)
   369	    }
   370	
   371	    /// Opens the `Path` with the given `OpenArgs`.
   372	    ///
   373	    /// Returns an `InodeHandle` on success.
   374	    pub fn open(&self, open_args: OpenArgs) -> Result<InodeHandle> {
   375	        let inode = self.inode();
   376	        let inode_type = inode.type_();
   377	        let creation_flags = &open_args.creation_flags;
   378	
   379	        match inode_type {
   380	            InodeType::NamedPipe => {
   381	                warn!("named pipes don't support additional operation when opening");
   382	                debug!("the named pipe is opened with {:?}", open_args);
   383	            }
   384	            InodeType::SymLink => {
   385	                if creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_NOFOLLOW)
   386	                    && !open_args.status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_PATH)
   387	                {
   388	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ELOOP, "the file is a symlink");
   389	                }
   390	            }
   391	            _ => {}
   392	        }
   393	
   394	        if creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_CREAT)
   395	            && creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_EXCL)
   396	        {
   397	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EEXIST, "the file already exists");
   398	        }
   399	        if creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_DIRECTORY) && inode_type != InodeType::Dir {
   400	            return_errno_with_message!(
   401	                Errno::ENOTDIR,
   402	                "O_DIRECTORY is specified but the file is not a directory"
   403	            );
   404	        }
   405	
   406	        if inode_type.is_regular_file() && creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_TRUNC) {
   407	            self.resize(0)?;
   408	        }
   409	        InodeHandle::new(self.clone(), open_args.access_mode, open_args.status_flags)
   410	    }
   411	}

   563	    fn read_direct_at(&self, offset: usize, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
   564	        self.read_at(offset, writer)
   565	    }
   566	
   567	    fn write_at(&self, offset: usize, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
   568	        let written_len = match self.typ {
   569	            InodeType::File => {
   570	                let page_cache = self.inner.as_file().unwrap();
   571	
   572	                let file_size = self.size();
   573	                let write_len = reader.remain();
   574	                let new_size = offset + write_len;
   575	                let should_expand_size = new_size > file_size;
   576	                let new_size_aligned = new_size.align_up(BLOCK_SIZE);
   577	                if should_expand_size {
   578	                    page_cache.resize(new_size_aligned)?;
   579	                }
   580	                page_cache.pages().write(offset, reader)?;
   581	
   582	                let now = now();
   583	                let mut inode_meta = self.metadata.lock();
   584	                inode_meta.set_mtime(now);
   585	                inode_meta.set_ctime(now);
   586	                if should_expand_size {
   587	                    inode_meta.size = new_size;
   588	                    inode_meta.blocks = new_size_aligned / BLOCK_SIZE;
   589	                }
   590	                write_len
   591	            }
   592	            InodeType::CharDevice | InodeType::BlockDevice => {
   593	                let device = self.inner.as_device().unwrap();
   594	                device.write(reader)?
   595	                // Typically, devices like "/dev/zero" or "/dev/null" do not require modifying
   596	                // timestamps here. Please adjust this behavior accordingly if there are special devices.
   597	            }
   598	            InodeType::NamedPipe => {
   599	                let named_pipe = self.inner.as_named_pipe().unwrap();
   600	                named_pipe.write(reader)?
   601	            }
   602	            _ => return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "write is not supported"),
   603	        };
   604	        Ok(written_len)
   605	    }
   606	
   607	    fn write_direct_at(&self, offset: usize, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
   608	        self.write_at(offset, reader)
   609	    }
   610	
   611	    fn size(&self) -> usize {
   612	        self.metadata.lock().size
   613	    }
   614	
   615	    fn resize(&self, new_size: usize) -> Result<()> {
   616	        if self.typ != InodeType::File {
   617	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "not regular file");
   618	        }
   619	
   620	        let file_size = self.size();
   621	        if file_size == new_size {
   622	            return Ok(());
   623	        }
   624	
   625	        let page_cache = self.inner.as_file().unwrap();
   626	        page_cache.resize(new_size)?;
   627	
   628	        let now = now();
   629	        let mut inode_meta = self.metadata.lock();
   630	        inode_meta.set_mtime(now);
   631	        inode_meta.set_ctime(now);
   632	        inode_meta.resize(new_size);
   633	        Ok(())
   634	    }

   638	    }

   690	    fn group(&self) -> Result<Gid> {
   691	        Ok(self.metadata.lock().gid)
   692	    }
   693	
   694	    fn set_group(&self, gid: Gid) -> Result<()> {
   695	        let mut inode_meta = self.metadata.lock();
   696	        inode_meta.gid = gid;
   697	        inode_meta.set_ctime(now());
   698	        Ok(())
   699	    }
   700	
   701	    fn mknod(&self, name: &str, mode: InodeMode, type_: MknodType) -> Result<Arc<dyn Inode>> {
   702	        if name.len() > NAME_MAX {
   703	            return_errno!(Errno::ENAMETOOLONG);
   704	        }
   705	        if self.typ != InodeType::Dir {
   706	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "self is not dir");
   707	        }
   708	
   709	        let self_dir = self.inner.as_direntry().unwrap().upread();
   710	        if self_dir.contains_entry(name) {
   800	
   801	        self.set_atime(now());
   802	
   803	        Ok(cnt)
   804	    }
   805	
   806	    fn link(&self, old: &Arc<dyn Inode>, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
   807	        if !Arc::ptr_eq(&self.fs(), &old.fs()) {
   808	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EXDEV, "not same fs");
   809	        }
   810	        if self.typ != InodeType::Dir {
   811	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "self is not dir");
   812	        }
   813	
   814	        let old = old
   815	            .downcast_ref::<RamInode>()
   816	            .ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EXDEV))?;
   817	        if old.typ == InodeType::Dir {
   818	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPERM, "old is a dir");
   819	        }
   820	
   821	        let mut self_dir = self.inner.as_direntry().unwrap().write();
   822	        if self_dir.contains_entry(name) {
   823	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EEXIST, "entry exist");
   824	        }
   825	        self_dir.append_entry(name, old.this.upgrade().unwrap());
   826	        drop(self_dir);
   827	
   828	        let now = now();
   829	        let mut self_meta = self.metadata.lock();
   830	        self_meta.set_mtime(now);
   831	        self_meta.set_ctime(now);
   832	        self_meta.inc_size();
   833	        drop(self_meta);
   834	
   835	        let mut old_meta = old.metadata.lock();
   836	        old_meta.inc_nlinks();
   837	        old_meta.set_ctime(now);
   838	
   839	        Ok(())
   840	    }
   841	
   842	    fn unlink(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
   843	        if is_dot_or_dotdot(name) {
   844	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "unlink . or ..");
   845	        }
   846	
   847	        let target = self.find(name)?;
   848	        if target.typ == InodeType::Dir {
   849	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "unlink on dir");
   850	        }
   851	
   852	        // When we got the lock, the dir may have been modified by another thread
   853	        let mut self_dir = self.inner.as_direntry().unwrap().write();
   854	        let (idx, new_target) = self_dir.get_entry(name).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::ENOENT))?;
   855	        if !Arc::ptr_eq(&new_target, &target) {
   856	            return_errno!(Errno::ENOENT);
   857	        }
   858	        self_dir.remove_entry(idx);
   859	        drop(self_dir);
   860	
   861	        let now = now();
   862	        let mut self_meta = self.metadata.lock();
   863	        self_meta.dec_size();
   864	        self_meta.set_mtime(now);
   865	        self_meta.set_ctime(now);
   866	        drop(self_meta);
   867	        let mut target_meta = target.metadata.lock();
   868	        target_meta.dec_nlinks();
   869	        target_meta.set_ctime(now);
   870	
   871	        Ok(())
   872	    }
   873	
   874	    fn rmdir(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
   875	        if is_dot(name) {
   876	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "rmdir on .");
   877	        }
   878	        if is_dotdot(name) {
   879	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTEMPTY, "rmdir on ..");
   880	        }
   881	
   882	        let target = self.find(name)?;
   883	        if target.typ != InodeType::Dir {
   884	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "rmdir on not dir");
   885	        }
   886	
   887	        // When we got the lock, the dir may have been modified by another thread
   888	        let (mut self_dir, target_dir) = write_lock_two_direntries_by_ino(
   889	            (self.ino, self.inner.as_direntry().unwrap()),
   890	            (target.ino, target.inner.as_direntry().unwrap()),
   891	        );
   892	        if !target_dir.is_empty_children() {
   893	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTEMPTY, "dir not empty");
   894	        }
   895	        let (idx, new_target) = self_dir.get_entry(name).ok_or(Error::new(Errno::ENOENT))?;
   896	        if !Arc::ptr_eq(&new_target, &target) {
   897	            return_errno!(Errno::ENOENT);
   898	        }
   899	        self_dir.remove_entry(idx);
   900	        drop(self_dir);
   901	        drop(target_dir);
   902	
   903	        let now = now();
   904	        let mut self_meta = self.metadata.lock();
   905	        self_meta.dec_size();
   906	        self_meta.dec_nlinks();
   907	        self_meta.set_mtime(now);
   908	        self_meta.set_ctime(now);
   909	        drop(self_meta);
   910	        let mut target_meta = target.metadata.lock();
   911	        target_meta.dec_nlinks();
   912	        target_meta.dec_nlinks();
   913	
   914	        Ok(())
   915	    }
   916	
   917	    fn lookup(&self, name: &str) -> Result<Arc<dyn Inode>> {
   918	        let inode = self.find(name)?;
   919	        Ok(inode as _)
   920	    }
   921	
   922	    fn rename(&self, old_name: &str, target: &Arc<dyn Inode>, new_name: &str) -> Result<()> {
   923	        if is_dot_or_dotdot(old_name) {
   924	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "old_name is . or ..");
   925	        }
   926	        if is_dot_or_dotdot(new_name) {
   927	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "new_name is . or ..");
   928	        }
   929	
   930	        let target = target
   931	            .downcast_ref::<RamInode>()
   932	            .ok_or(Error::new(Errno::EXDEV))?;
   933	
   934	        if !Arc::ptr_eq(&self.fs(), &target.fs()) {
   935	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EXDEV, "not same fs");
   936	        }
   937	        if self.typ != InodeType::Dir {
   938	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "self is not dir");
   939	        }
   940	        if target.typ != InodeType::Dir {
   941	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "target is not dir");
   942	        }
   943	
   944	        // Perform necessary checks to ensure that `dst_inode` can be replaced by `src_inode`.
   945	        let check_replace_inode =
   946	            |src_inode: &Arc<RamInode>, dst_inode: &Arc<RamInode>| -> Result<()> {
   947	                if src_inode.ino == dst_inode.ino {
   948	                    return Ok(());
   949	                }
   950	
   951	                match (src_inode.typ, dst_inode.typ) {
   952	                    (InodeType::Dir, InodeType::Dir) => {
   953	                        if !dst_inode
   954	                            .inner
   955	                            .as_direntry()
   956	                            .unwrap()
   957	                            .read()
   958	                            .is_empty_children()
   959	                        {
   960	                            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTEMPTY, "dir not empty");
   961	                        }
   962	                    }
   963	                    (InodeType::Dir, _) => {
   964	                        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "old is not dir");
   965	                    }
   966	                    (_, InodeType::Dir) => {
   967	                        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "new is dir");
   968	                    }
   969	                    _ => {}
   970	                }
   971	                Ok(())
   972	            };
   973	
   974	        // Rename in the same directory
   975	        if self.ino == target.ino {
   976	            let mut self_dir = self.inner.as_direntry().unwrap().write();
   977	            let (src_idx, src_inode) = self_dir
   978	                .get_entry(old_name)
   979	                .ok_or(Error::new(Errno::ENOENT))?;
   980	            let is_dir = src_inode.typ == InodeType::Dir;
   981	
   982	            if let Some((dst_idx, dst_inode)) = self_dir.get_entry(new_name) {
   983	                check_replace_inode(&src_inode, &dst_inode)?;
   984	                self_dir.remove_entry(dst_idx);
   985	                self_dir.substitute_entry(src_idx, (CStr256::from(new_name), src_inode.clone()));
   986	                drop(self_dir);
   987	
   988	                let now = now();
   989	                let mut self_meta = self.metadata.lock();
   990	                self_meta.dec_size();
   991	                if is_dir {
   992	                    self_meta.dec_nlinks();
   993	                }
   994	                self_meta.set_mtime(now);
   995	                self_meta.set_ctime(now);
   996	                drop(self_meta);
   997	                src_inode.set_ctime(now);
   998	                dst_inode.set_ctime(now);
   999	            } else {
  1000	                self_dir.substitute_entry(src_idx, (CStr256::from(new_name), src_inode.clone()));
  1001	                drop(self_dir);
  1002	                let now = now();
  1003	                let mut self_meta = self.metadata.lock();
  1004	                self_meta.set_mtime(now);
  1005	                self_meta.set_ctime(now);
  1006	                drop(self_meta);
  1007	                src_inode.set_ctime(now);
  1008	            }
  1009	        }
  1010	        // Or rename across different directories
  1011	        else {
  1012	            let (mut self_dir, mut target_dir) = write_lock_two_direntries_by_ino(
  1013	                (self.ino, self.inner.as_direntry().unwrap()),
  1014	                (target.ino, target.inner.as_direntry().unwrap()),
  1015	            );
  1016	            let self_inode_arc = self.this.upgrade().unwrap();
  1017	            let target_inode_arc = target.this.upgrade().unwrap();
  1018	            let (src_idx, src_inode) = self_dir
  1019	                .get_entry(old_name)
  1020	                .ok_or(Error::new(Errno::ENOENT))?;
  1021	            // Avoid renaming a directory to a subdirectory of itself
  1022	            if Arc::ptr_eq(&src_inode, &target_inode_arc) {
  1023	                return_errno!(Errno::EINVAL);
  1024	            }
  1025	            let is_dir = src_inode.typ == InodeType::Dir;
  1026	
  1027	            if let Some((dst_idx, dst_inode)) = target_dir.get_entry(new_name) {
  1028	                // Avoid renaming a subdirectory to a directory.
  1029	                if Arc::ptr_eq(&self_inode_arc, &dst_inode) {
  1030	                    return_errno!(Errno::ENOTEMPTY);
  1031	                }
  1032	                check_replace_inode(&src_inode, &dst_inode)?;
  1033	                self_dir.remove_entry(src_idx);
  1034	                target_dir.remove_entry(dst_idx);
  1035	                target_dir.append_entry(new_name, src_inode.clone());
  1036	                drop(self_dir);
  1037	                drop(target_dir);
  1038	
  1039	                let now = now();
  1040	                let mut self_meta = self.metadata.lock();
  1041	                self_meta.dec_size();
  1042	                if is_dir {
  1043	                    self_meta.dec_nlinks();
  1044	                }
  1045	                self_meta.set_mtime(now);
  1046	                self_meta.set_ctime(now);
  1047	                drop(self_meta);
  1048	                let mut target_meta = target.metadata.lock();
  1049	                target_meta.set_mtime(now);
  1050	                target_meta.set_ctime(now);
  1051	                drop(target_meta);
  1052	                dst_inode.set_ctime(now);
  1053	                src_inode.set_ctime(now);
  1054	            } else {
  1055	                self_dir.remove_entry(src_idx);
  1056	                target_dir.append_entry(new_name, src_inode.clone());
  1057	                drop(self_dir);
  1058	                drop(target_dir);
  1059	
  1060	                let now = now();
  1061	                let mut self_meta = self.metadata.lock();
  1062	                self_meta.dec_size();
  1063	                if is_dir {
  1064	                    self_meta.dec_nlinks();
  1065	                }
  1066	                self_meta.set_mtime(now);
  1067	                self_meta.set_ctime(now);
  1068	                drop(self_meta);
  1069	
  1070	                let mut target_meta = target.metadata.lock();
  1120	            .map(|device| device.id().as_encoded_u64())
  1121	            .unwrap_or(0);
  1122	        let inode_metadata = self.metadata.lock();
  1123	        Metadata {
  1124	            dev: 0,
  1125	            ino: self.ino as _,
  1126	            size: inode_metadata.size,
  1127	            blk_size: BLOCK_SIZE,
  1128	            blocks: inode_metadata.blocks,
  1129	            atime: inode_metadata.atime,
  1130	            mtime: inode_metadata.mtime,
  1131	            ctime: inode_metadata.ctime,
  1132	            type_: self.typ,
  1133	            mode: inode_metadata.mode,
  1134	            nlinks: inode_metadata.nlinks,
  1135	            uid: inode_metadata.uid,
  1136	            gid: inode_metadata.gid,
  1137	            rdev,
  1138	        }
  1139	    }
  1140	
  1141	    fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
  1142	        if !self.typ.is_device() {
  1143	            return (IoEvents::IN | IoEvents::OUT) & mask;
  1144	        }
  1145	
  1146	        let device = self
  1147	            .inner
  1148	            .as_device()
  1149	            .expect("[Internal error] self.typ is device, while self.inner is not");
  1150	        device.poll(mask, poller)
  1151	    }
  1152	
  1153	    fn fs(&self) -> Arc<dyn FileSystem> {
  1154	        Weak::upgrade(&self.fs).unwrap()
  1155	    }
  1156	
  1157	    fn fallocate(&self, mode: FallocMode, offset: usize, len: usize) -> Result<()> {
  1158	        if self.typ != InodeType::File {
  1159	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "not regular file");
  1160	        }

    33	#[derive(Debug)]
    34	pub struct InodeHandle<R = Rights>(Arc<InodeHandle_>, R);
    35	
    36	struct InodeHandle_ {
    37	    path: Path,
    38	    /// `file_io` is Similar to `file_private` field in `file` structure in linux. If
    39	    /// `file_io` is Some, typical file operations including `read`, `write`, `poll`,
    40	    /// `ioctl` will be provided by `file_io`, instead of `path`.
    41	    file_io: Option<Arc<dyn FileIo>>,
    42	    offset: Mutex<usize>,
    43	    access_mode: AccessMode,
    44	    status_flags: AtomicU32,
    45	}
    46	
    47	impl InodeHandle_ {
    48	    pub fn read(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
    49	        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
    50	            return file_io.read(writer);
    51	        }
    52	
    53	        if !self.path.inode().is_seekable() {
    54	            return self.read_at(0, writer);
    55	        }
    56	
    57	        let mut offset = self.offset.lock();
    58	
    59	        let len = self.read_at(*offset, writer)?;
    60	
    61	        *offset += len;
    62	        Ok(len)
    63	    }
    64	
    65	    pub fn write(&self, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
    66	        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
    67	            return file_io.write(reader);
    68	        }
    69	
    70	        if !self.path.inode().is_seekable() {
    71	            return self.write_at(0, reader);
    72	        }
    73	
    74	        let mut offset = self.offset.lock();
    75	
    76	        if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND) {
    77	            *offset = self.path.size();
    78	        }
    79	
    80	        let len = self.write_at(*offset, reader)?;
    81	
    82	        *offset += len;
    83	        Ok(len)
    84	    }
    85	
    86	    pub fn read_at(&self, offset: usize, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
    87	        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
    88	            todo!("support read_at for FileIo");
    89	        }
    90	
    91	        if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_DIRECT) {
    92	            self.path.inode().read_direct_at(offset, writer)
    93	        } else {
    94	            self.path.inode().read_at(offset, writer)
    95	        }
    96	    }
    97	
    98	    pub fn write_at(&self, mut offset: usize, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
    99	        if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
   100	            todo!("support write_at for FileIo");
   101	        }
   102	
   103	        let status_flags = self.status_flags();
   104	        if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND) {
   105	            // If the file has the O_APPEND flag, the offset is ignored
   106	            offset = self.path.size();
   107	        }
   108	
   109	        if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_DIRECT) {
   110	            self.path.inode().write_direct_at(offset, reader)
   111	        } else {
   112	            self.path.inode().write_at(offset, reader)
   113	        }
   114	    }
   115	
   116	    pub fn seek(&self, pos: SeekFrom) -> Result<usize> {
   117	        do_seek_util(self.path.inode(), &self.offset, pos)
   118	    }
   119	
   120	    pub fn offset(&self) -> usize {
   121	        let offset = self.offset.lock();
   122	        *offset
   123	    }
   124	
   125	    pub fn resize(&self, new_size: usize) -> Result<()> {
   126	        do_resize_util(self.path.inode(), self.status_flags(), new_size)
   127	    }
   128	
   129	    pub fn access_mode(&self) -> AccessMode {
   130	        self.access_mode
   390	}
   391	
   392	pub fn do_fallocate_util(
   393	    inode: &Arc<dyn Inode>,
   394	    status_flags: StatusFlags,
   395	    mode: FallocMode,
   396	    offset: usize,
   397	    len: usize,
   398	) -> Result<()> {
   399	    if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND)
   400	        && (mode == FallocMode::PunchHoleKeepSize
   401	            || mode == FallocMode::CollapseRange
   402	            || mode == FallocMode::InsertRange)
   403	    {
   404	        return_errno_with_message!(
   405	            Errno::EPERM,
   406	            "the flags do not work on the append-only file"
   407	        );
   408	    }
   409	    if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_DIRECT) || status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_PATH) {
   410	        return_errno_with_message!(
   411	            Errno::EBADF,
   412	            "currently fallocate file with O_DIRECT or O_PATH is not supported"
   413	        );
   414	    }
   415	
   416	    inode.fallocate(mode, offset, len)
   417	}
   418	
   419	pub fn do_resize_util(
   420	    inode: &Arc<dyn Inode>,
   421	    status_flags: StatusFlags,
   422	    new_size: usize,
   423	) -> Result<()> {
   424	    if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND) {
   425	        // FIXME: It's allowed to `ftruncate` an append-only file on Linux.
   426	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPERM, "can not resize append-only file");
   427	    }
   428	    inode.resize(new_size)
   429	}

    14	pub struct NamedPipe {
    15	    reader: Arc<PipeReader>,
    16	    writer: Arc<PipeWriter>,
    17	}
    18	
    19	impl NamedPipe {
    20	    pub fn new() -> Result<Self> {
    21	        let (reader, writer) = pipe::new_pair()?;
    22	
    23	        Ok(Self { reader, writer })
    24	    }
    25	}
    26	
    27	impl Pollable for NamedPipe {
    28	    fn poll(&self, _mask: IoEvents, _poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
    29	        warn!("Named pipe doesn't support poll now, return IoEvents::empty for now.");
    30	        IoEvents::empty()
    31	    }
    32	}
    33	
    34	impl FileLike for NamedPipe {
    35	    fn read(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
    36	        self.reader.read(writer)
    37	    }
    38	
    39	    fn write(&self, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
    40	        self.writer.write(reader)
    41	    }

    44	        AccessMode::O_RDWR
    45	    }

     5	use super::{
     6	    file_handle::FileLike,
     7	    utils::{mkmod, AccessMode, Endpoint, EndpointState, InodeType, Metadata, StatusFlags},
     8	};
     9	use crate::{
    10	    events::IoEvents,
    11	    prelude::*,
    12	    process::{
    13	        signal::{PollHandle, Pollable},
    14	        Gid, Uid,
    15	    },
    16	    time::clocks::RealTimeCoarseClock,
    17	    util::ring_buffer::{RbConsumer, RbProducer, RingBuffer},
    18	};
    19	
    20	const DEFAULT_PIPE_BUF_SIZE: usize = 65536;
    21	
    22	/// Maximum number of bytes guaranteed to be written to a pipe atomically.
    23	///
    24	/// If the number of bytes to be written is less than the threshold, the write must be atomic.
    25	/// A non-blocking atomic write may fail with `EAGAIN`, even if there is room for a partial write.
    26	/// In other words, a partial write is not allowed for an atomic write.
    27	///
    28	/// For more details, see the description of `PIPE_BUF` in
    29	/// <https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/pipe.7.html>.
    30	#[cfg(not(ktest))]
    31	const PIPE_BUF: usize = 4096;
    32	#[cfg(ktest)]
    33	const PIPE_BUF: usize = 2;
    34	
    35	pub fn new_pair() -> Result<(Arc<PipeReader>, Arc<PipeWriter>)> {
    36	    new_pair_with_capacity(DEFAULT_PIPE_BUF_SIZE)
    37	}
    38	
    39	fn new_pair_with_capacity(capacity: usize) -> Result<(Arc<PipeReader>, Arc<PipeWriter>)> {
    40	    let (producer, consumer) = RingBuffer::new(capacity).split();
    41	    let (producer_state, consumer_state) =
    42	        Endpoint::new_pair(EndpointState::default(), EndpointState::default());
    43	
    44	    Ok((
    45	        PipeReader::new(consumer, consumer_state, StatusFlags::empty())?,
    46	        PipeWriter::new(producer, producer_state, StatusFlags::empty())?,
    47	    ))
    48	}
    49	
    50	pub struct PipeReader {
    51	    consumer: Mutex<RbConsumer<u8>>,
    52	    state: Endpoint<EndpointState>,
    53	    status_flags: AtomicU32,
    54	}
    55	
    56	impl PipeReader {
    57	    fn new(
    58	        consumer: RbConsumer<u8>,
    59	        state: Endpoint<EndpointState>,
    60	        status_flags: StatusFlags,
    61	    ) -> Result<Arc<Self>> {
    62	        check_status_flags(status_flags)?;
    63	
    64	        Ok(Arc::new(Self {
    65	            consumer: Mutex::new(consumer),
    66	            state,
    67	            status_flags: AtomicU32::new(status_flags.bits()),
    68	        }))
    69	    }
    70	
    71	    fn try_read(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
    72	        let read = || {
    73	            let mut consumer = self.consumer.lock();
    74	            consumer.read_fallible(writer)
    75	        };
    76	
    77	        self.state.read_with(read)
    78	    }
    79	
    80	    fn check_io_events(&self) -> IoEvents {
    81	        let mut events = IoEvents::empty();
    82	        if self.state.is_peer_shutdown() {
    83	            events |= IoEvents::HUP;
    84	        }
    85	        if !self.consumer.lock().is_empty() {
    86	            events |= IoEvents::IN;
    87	        }
    88	        events
    89	    }
    90	}
    91	
    92	impl Pollable for PipeReader {
    93	    fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
    94	        self.state
    95	            .poll_with(mask, poller, || self.check_io_events())
    96	    }
    97	}
    98	
    99	impl FileLike for PipeReader {
   100	    fn read(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
   101	        if !writer.has_avail() {
   102	            // Even the peer endpoint (`PipeWriter`) has been closed, reading an empty buffer is
   103	            // still fine.
   104	            return Ok(0);
   105	        }
   106	
   107	        if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_NONBLOCK) {
   108	            self.try_read(writer)
   109	        } else {
   110	            self.wait_events(IoEvents::IN, None, || self.try_read(writer))
   111	        }
   112	    }
   113	
   114	    fn status_flags(&self) -> StatusFlags {
   115	        StatusFlags::from_bits_truncate(self.status_flags.load(Ordering::Relaxed))
   116	    }
   117	
   118	    fn set_status_flags(&self, new_flags: StatusFlags) -> Result<()> {
   119	        check_status_flags(new_flags)?;
   120	
   121	        self.status_flags.store(new_flags.bits(), Ordering::Relaxed);
   122	        Ok(())
   123	    }
   124	
   125	    fn access_mode(&self) -> AccessMode {
   126	        AccessMode::O_RDONLY
   127	    }
   128	
   129	    fn metadata(&self) -> Metadata {
   130	        // This is a dummy implementation.
   131	        // TODO: Add "PipeFS" and link `PipeReader` to it.
   132	        let now = RealTimeCoarseClock::get().read_time();
   133	        Metadata {
   134	            dev: 0,
   135	            ino: 0,
   136	            size: 0,
   137	            blk_size: 0,
   138	            blocks: 0,
   139	            atime: now,
   140	            mtime: now,
   141	            ctime: now,
   142	            type_: InodeType::NamedPipe,
   143	            mode: mkmod!(u+r),
   144	            nlinks: 1,
   145	            uid: Uid::new_root(),
   146	            gid: Gid::new_root(),
   147	            rdev: 0,
   148	        }
   149	    }
   150	}
   151	
   152	impl Drop for PipeReader {
   153	    fn drop(&mut self) {
   154	        self.state.peer_shutdown();
   155	    }
   156	}
   157	
   158	pub struct PipeWriter {
   159	    producer: Mutex<RbProducer<u8>>,
   160	    state: Endpoint<EndpointState>,
   161	    status_flags: AtomicU32,
   162	}
   163	
   164	impl PipeWriter {
   165	    fn new(
   166	        producer: RbProducer<u8>,
   167	        state: Endpoint<EndpointState>,
   168	        status_flags: StatusFlags,
   169	    ) -> Result<Arc<Self>> {
   170	        check_status_flags(status_flags)?;
   171	
   172	        Ok(Arc::new(Self {
   173	            producer: Mutex::new(producer),
   174	            state,
   175	            status_flags: AtomicU32::new(status_flags.bits()),
   176	        }))
   177	    }
   178	
   179	    fn try_write(&self, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
   180	        let write = || {

    13	pub fn sys_fchmod(fd: FileDesc, mode: u16, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    14	    debug!("fd = {}, mode = 0o{:o}", fd, mode);
    15	
    16	    let mut file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table_mut();
    17	    let file = get_file_fast!(&mut file_table, fd);
    18	    file.set_mode(InodeMode::from_bits_truncate(mode))?;
    19	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    20	}

    26	// Glibc handles the `flags` argument, so we just ignore it.
    27	pub fn sys_fchmodat(
    28	    dirfd: FileDesc,
    29	    path_ptr: Vaddr,
    30	    mode: u16,
    31	    /* flags: u32, */
    32	    ctx: &Context,
    33	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    34	    let path_name = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_ptr, PATH_MAX)?;
    35	    debug!(
    36	        "dirfd = {}, path_name = {:?}, mode = 0o{:o}",
    37	        dirfd, path_name, mode,
    38	    );
    39	
    40	    let path = {
    41	        let path_name = path_name.to_string_lossy();
    42	        let fs_path = FsPath::from_fd_and_path(dirfd, &path_name)?;
    43	        ctx.thread_local
    44	            .borrow_fs()
    45	            .resolver()
    46	            .read()
    47	            .lookup(&fs_path)?
    48	    };
    49	
    50	    path.set_mode(InodeMode::from_bits_truncate(mode))?;
    51	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    52	}
