    66	pub fn sys_fchown(fd: FileDesc, uid: i32, gid: i32, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    67	    debug!("fd = {}, uid = {}, gid = {}", fd, uid, gid);
    68	
    69	    let uid = to_optional_id(uid, Uid::new)?;
    70	    let gid = to_optional_id(gid, Gid::new)?;
    71	    if uid.is_none() && gid.is_none() {
    72	        return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0));
    73	    }
    74	
    75	    let mut file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table_mut();
    76	    let file = get_file_fast!(&mut file_table, fd);
    77	    if let Some(uid) = uid {
    78	        file.set_owner(uid)?;
    79	    }
    80	    if let Some(gid) = gid {
    81	        file.set_group(gid)?;
    82	    }
    83	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    84	}

    86	pub fn sys_chown(path_ptr: Vaddr, uid: i32, gid: i32, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {

    88	}

    90	pub fn sys_lchown(path_ptr: Vaddr, uid: i32, gid: i32, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    91	    self::sys_fchownat(
    92	        AT_FDCWD,
    93	        path_ptr,
    94	        uid,
    95	        gid,
    96	        ChownFlags::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW.bits(),
    97	        ctx,
    98	    )
    99	}
   100	
   101	pub fn sys_fchownat(
   102	    dirfd: FileDesc,
   103	    path_ptr: Vaddr,
   104	    uid: i32,
   105	    gid: i32,
   106	    flags: u32,
   107	    ctx: &Context,
   108	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
   109	    let path_name = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_ptr, PATH_MAX)?;
   110	    let flags = ChownFlags::from_bits(flags)
   111	        .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid flags"))?;
   112	    debug!(
   113	        "dirfd = {}, path = {:?}, uid = {}, gid = {}, flags = {:?}",
   114	        dirfd, path_name, uid, gid, flags
   115	    );
   116	
   117	    if flags.contains(ChownFlags::AT_EMPTY_PATH) && path_name.is_empty() {
   118	        return self::sys_fchown(dirfd, uid, gid, ctx);
   119	    }
   120	
   121	    let uid = to_optional_id(uid, Uid::new)?;
   122	    let gid = to_optional_id(gid, Gid::new)?;
   123	    if uid.is_none() && gid.is_none() {
   124	        return Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0));
   125	    }
   126	
   127	    let path = {
   128	        let path_name = path_name.to_string_lossy();
   129	        let fs_path = FsPath::from_fd_and_path(dirfd, &path_name)?;
   130	
   131	        let fs_ref = ctx.thread_local.borrow_fs();
   132	        let fs = fs_ref.resolver().read();
   133	        if flags.contains(ChownFlags::AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW) {
   134	            fs.lookup_no_follow(&fs_path)?
   135	        } else {
   136	            fs.lookup(&fs_path)?
   137	        }
   138	    };
   139	
   140	    if let Some(uid) = uid {
   141	        path.set_owner(uid)?;
   142	    }
   143	    if let Some(gid) = gid {
   144	        path.set_group(gid)?;
   145	    }
   146	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
   147	}
   148	
   149	fn to_optional_id<T>(id: i32, f: impl Fn(u32) -> T) -> Result<Option<T>> {
   150	    let id = if id >= 0 {
   151	        Some(f(id as u32))
   152	    } else if id == -1 {
   153	        // If the owner or group is specified as -1, then that ID is not changed.
   154	        None
   155	    } else {
   156	        return_errno!(Errno::EINVAL);
   157	    };
   158	
   159	    Ok(id)
   160	}
   161	
   162	bitflags! {
   163	    struct ChownFlags: u32 {
   164	        const AT_SYMLINK_NOFOLLOW = 1 << 8;
   165	        const AT_EMPTY_PATH = 1 << 12;
   166	    }
   167	}

   181	pub fn sys_ftruncate(fd: FileDesc, len: isize, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
   182	    debug!("fd = {}, length = {}", fd, len);
   183	
   184	    check_length(len, ctx)?;
   185	
   186	    let mut file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table_mut();
   187	    let file = get_file_fast!(&mut file_table, fd);
   188	    file.resize(len as usize)?;
   189	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
   190	}
   191	
   192	pub fn sys_truncate(path_ptr: Vaddr, len: isize, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
   193	    let path_name = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_ptr, PATH_MAX)?;
   194	    debug!("path = {:?}, length = {}", path_name, len);
   195	
   196	    check_length(len, ctx)?;
   197	
   198	    let dir_path = {
   199	        let path_name = path_name.to_string_lossy();
   200	        let fs_path = FsPath::from_fd_and_path(AT_FDCWD, &path_name)?;
   201	        ctx.thread_local
   202	            .borrow_fs()
   203	            .resolver()
   204	            .read()
   205	            .lookup(&fs_path)?
   206	    };
   207	    dir_path.resize(len as usize)?;
   208	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
   209	}
   210	
   211	#[inline]
   212	fn check_length(len: isize, ctx: &Context) -> Result<()> {
   213	    if len < 0 {
   214	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "length is negative");
   215	    }
   216	
   217	    let max_file_size = {
   218	        let resource_limits = ctx.process.resource_limits();
   219	        resource_limits
   220	            .get_rlimit(ResourceType::RLIMIT_FSIZE)
   221	            .get_cur() as usize
   222	    };
   223	    if len as usize > max_file_size {
   224	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EFBIG, "length is larger than the maximum file size");
   225	    }
   226	    Ok(())
   227	}

     3	use super::SyscallReturn;
     4	use crate::{
     5	    fs::{
     6	        file_table::FileDesc,

    13	
    14	pub fn sys_mkdirat(
    15	    dirfd: FileDesc,
    16	    path_addr: Vaddr,
    17	    mode: u16,
    18	    ctx: &Context,
    19	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    20	    let path = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_addr, MAX_FILENAME_LEN)?;
    21	    debug!("dirfd = {}, path = {:?}, mode = {}", dirfd, path, mode);
    22	
    23	    let fs_ref = ctx.thread_local.borrow_fs();
    24	    let (dir_path, name) = {
    25	        let path = path.to_string_lossy();
    26	        let fs_path = FsPath::from_fd_and_path(dirfd, &path)?;
    27	        fs_ref
    28	            .resolver()
    29	            .read()
    30	            .lookup_unresolved_no_follow(&fs_path)?
    31	            .into_parent_and_basename()?
    32	    };
    33	
    34	    let inode_mode = {
    35	        let mask_mode = mode & !fs_ref.umask().get();
    36	        InodeMode::from_bits_truncate(mask_mode)
    37	    };
    38	    let _ = dir_path.new_fs_child(&name, InodeType::Dir, inode_mode)?;
    39	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
    40	}

    53	        utils::{AccessMode, CreationFlags, InodeMode, InodeType, OpenArgs},
    54	    },
    55	    prelude::*,
    56	    syscall::constants::MAX_FILENAME_LEN,
    57	};
    58	
    59	pub fn sys_openat(
    60	    dirfd: FileDesc,
    61	    path_addr: Vaddr,
    62	    flags: u32,
    63	    mode: u16,
    64	    ctx: &Context,
    65	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
    66	    let path = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_addr, MAX_FILENAME_LEN)?;
    67	    debug!(
    68	        "dirfd = {}, path = {:?}, flags = {}, mode = {}",
    69	        dirfd, path, flags, mode
    70	    );
    71	
    72	    let file_handle = {
    73	        let path = path.to_string_lossy();
    74	        let fs_path = FsPath::from_fd_and_path(dirfd, path.as_ref())?;
    75	
    76	        let fs_ref = ctx.thread_local.borrow_fs();
    77	        let mask_mode = mode & !fs_ref.umask().get();
    78	
    79	        let fs_resolver = fs_ref.resolver().read();
    80	        let inode_handle = do_open(
    81	            &fs_resolver,
    82	            &fs_path,
    83	            flags,
    84	            InodeMode::from_bits_truncate(mask_mode),
    85	        )
    86	        .map_err(|err| match err.error() {
    87	            Errno::EINTR => Error::new(Errno::ERESTARTSYS),
    88	            _ => err,
    89	        })?;
    90	
    91	        Arc::new(inode_handle)
    92	    };
    93	
    94	    let fd = {
    95	        let file_table = ctx.thread_local.borrow_file_table();
    96	        let mut file_table_locked = file_table.unwrap().write();
    97	        let fd_flags =
    98	            if CreationFlags::from_bits_truncate(flags).contains(CreationFlags::O_CLOEXEC) {
    99	                FdFlags::CLOEXEC
   100	            } else {
   101	                FdFlags::empty()
   102	            };
   103	        file_table_locked.insert(file_handle, fd_flags)
   104	    };
   105	
   106	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(fd as _))
   107	}
   108	
   109	pub fn sys_open(path_addr: Vaddr, flags: u32, mode: u16, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
   110	    self::sys_openat(AT_FDCWD, path_addr, flags, mode, ctx)
   111	}
   112	
   113	pub fn sys_creat(path_addr: Vaddr, mode: u16, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
   114	    let flags =
   115	        AccessMode::O_WRONLY as u32 | CreationFlags::O_CREAT.bits() | CreationFlags::O_TRUNC.bits();
   116	    self::sys_openat(AT_FDCWD, path_addr, flags, mode, ctx)
   117	}
   118	
   119	fn do_open(
   120	    fs_resolver: &FsResolver,
   121	    path: &FsPath,
   122	    flags: u32,
   123	    mode: InodeMode,
   124	) -> Result<InodeHandle> {
   125	    let open_args = OpenArgs::from_flags_and_mode(flags, mode)?;
   126	
   127	    let lookup_res = if open_args.follow_tail_link() {
   128	        fs_resolver.lookup_unresolved(path)?
   129	    } else {
   130	        fs_resolver.lookup_unresolved_no_follow(path)?
   131	    };
   132	
   133	    let inode_handle = match lookup_res {
   134	        LookupResult::Resolved(target_path) => target_path.open(open_args)?,
   135	        LookupResult::AtParent(result) => {
   136	            if !open_args.creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_CREAT) {
   137	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOENT, "the file does not exist");
   138	            }
   139	            if open_args
   140	                .creation_flags
   141	                .contains(CreationFlags::O_DIRECTORY)
   142	            {
   143	                return_errno_with_message!(
   144	                    Errno::EINVAL,
   145	                    "O_CREAT and O_DIRECTORY cannot be specified together"
   146	                );
   147	            }
   148	            if result.target_is_dir() {
   149	                return_errno_with_message!(
   150	                    Errno::EISDIR,
   151	                    "O_CREAT is specified but the file is a directory"
   152	                );
   153	            }
   154	
   155	            let (parent, tail_name) = result.into_parent_and_basename();
   156	            let new_path =
   157	                parent.new_fs_child(&tail_name, InodeType::File, open_args.inode_mode)?;
   158	
   159	            // Don't check access mode for newly created file.
   160	            InodeHandle::new_unchecked_access(
   161	                new_path,
   162	                open_args.access_mode,
   163	                open_args.status_flags,
   164	            )?
   165	        }
   166	    };
   167	
   168	    Ok(inode_handle)
   169	}
   170	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
   171	
   172	use super::SyscallReturn;
   173	use crate::{
   174	    device::get_device,
   175	    fs::{
   176	        device::DeviceId,
   177	        file_table::FileDesc,
   178	        fs_resolver::{FsPath, AT_FDCWD},
   179	        utils::{InodeMode, InodeType, MknodType},
   180	    },
   181	    prelude::*,
   182	    syscall::constants::MAX_FILENAME_LEN,
   183	};
   184	
   185	pub fn sys_mknodat(
   186	    dirfd: FileDesc,
   187	    path_addr: Vaddr,
   188	    mode: u16,
   189	    dev: usize,
   190	    ctx: &Context,
   191	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
   192	    let path_name = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_addr, MAX_FILENAME_LEN)?;
   193	    let fs_ref = ctx.thread_local.borrow_fs();
   194	    let inode_mode = {
   195	        let mask_mode = mode & !fs_ref.umask().get();
   196	        InodeMode::from_bits_truncate(mask_mode)
   197	    };
   198	    let inode_type = InodeType::from_raw_mode(mode)?;
   199	    debug!(
   200	        "dirfd = {}, path = {:?}, inode_mode = {:?}, inode_type = {:?}, dev = {}",
   201	        dirfd, path_name, inode_mode, inode_type, dev
   202	    );
   203	
   204	    let (dir_path, name) = {
   205	        let path_name = path_name.to_string_lossy();
   206	        let fs_path = FsPath::from_fd_and_path(dirfd, &path_name)?;
   207	        fs_ref
   208	            .resolver()
   209	            .read()
   210	            .lookup_unresolved_no_follow(&fs_path)?
   211	            .into_parent_and_filename()?
   212	    };
   213	
   214	    match inode_type {
   215	        InodeType::File => {
   216	            let _ = dir_path.new_fs_child(&name, InodeType::File, inode_mode)?;
   217	        }
   218	        InodeType::CharDevice | InodeType::BlockDevice => {
   219	            let device_inode = get_device(DeviceId::from_encoded_u64(dev as u64))?;
   220	            let _ = dir_path.mknod(&name, inode_mode, device_inode.into())?;
   221	        }
   222	        InodeType::NamedPipe => {
   223	            let _ = dir_path.mknod(&name, inode_mode, MknodType::NamedPipe)?;
   224	        }
   225	        InodeType::Socket => {
   226	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "unsupported file types")
   227	        }
   228	        _ => return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPERM, "unimplemented file types"),
   229	    }
   230	
   231	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
   232	}
   233	
   234	pub fn sys_mknod(path_addr: Vaddr, mode: u16, dev: usize, ctx: &Context) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
   235	    self::sys_mknodat(AT_FDCWD, path_addr, mode, dev, ctx)
   236	}

   238	
   239	use super::SyscallReturn;
   240	use crate::{
   241	    fs::{
   242	        file_table::FileDesc,
   243	        fs_resolver::{FsPath, AT_FDCWD},
   244	        utils::InodeType,
   245	    },
   246	    prelude::*,
   247	    syscall::constants::MAX_FILENAME_LEN,
   248	};
   249	
   250	pub fn sys_linkat(
   251	    old_dirfd: FileDesc,
   252	    old_path_addr: Vaddr,
   253	    new_dirfd: FileDesc,
   254	    new_path_addr: Vaddr,
   255	    flags: u32,
   256	    ctx: &Context,
   257	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
   258	    let user_space = ctx.user_space();
   259	
   260	    let old_path_name = user_space.read_cstring(old_path_addr, MAX_FILENAME_LEN)?;
   261	    let new_path_name = user_space.read_cstring(new_path_addr, MAX_FILENAME_LEN)?;
   262	    let flags: LinkFlags =
   263	        LinkFlags::from_bits(flags).ok_or(Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid flags"))?;
   264	    debug!(
   265	        "old_dirfd = {}, old_path = {:?}, new_dirfd = {}, new_path = {:?}, flags = {:?}",
   266	        old_dirfd, old_path_name, new_dirfd, new_path_name, flags
   267	    );
   268	
   269	    let (old_path, new_path, new_name) = {
   270	        let old_path_name = old_path_name.to_string_lossy();
   271	        let new_path_name = new_path_name.to_string_lossy();
   272	
   273	        let old_fs_path = if flags.contains(LinkFlags::AT_EMPTY_PATH) && old_path_name.is_empty() {
   274	            FsPath::from_fd(old_dirfd)?
   275	        } else {
   276	            FsPath::from_fd_and_path(old_dirfd, &old_path_name)?
   277	        };
   278	        let new_fs_path = FsPath::from_fd_and_path(new_dirfd, &new_path_name)?;
   279	
   280	        let fs_ref = ctx.thread_local.borrow_fs();
   281	        let fs = fs_ref.resolver().read();
   282	
   283	        let old_path = if flags.contains(LinkFlags::AT_SYMLINK_FOLLOW) {
   284	            fs.lookup(&old_fs_path)?
   285	        } else {
   286	            fs.lookup_no_follow(&old_fs_path)?
   287	        };
   288	        if old_path.type_() == InodeType::Dir {
   289	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPERM, "the link path is a directory");
   290	        }
   291	
   292	        let (new_path, new_name) = fs
   293	            .lookup_unresolved_no_follow(&new_fs_path)?
   294	            .into_parent_and_filename()?;
   295	
   296	        (old_path, new_path, new_name)
   297	    };
   298	
   299	    new_path.link(&old_path, &new_name)?;
   300	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
   301	}
   302	
   303	pub fn sys_link(
   304	    old_path_addr: Vaddr,
   305	    new_path_addr: Vaddr,
   306	    ctx: &Context,
   307	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
   308	    self::sys_linkat(AT_FDCWD, old_path_addr, AT_FDCWD, new_path_addr, 0, ctx)
   309	}
   310	
   311	bitflags::bitflags! {
   312	    pub struct LinkFlags: u32 {
   313	        const AT_EMPTY_PATH = 0x1000;
   314	        const AT_SYMLINK_FOLLOW = 0x400;
   315	    }
   316	}
   317	// SPDX-License-Identifier: MPL-2.0
   318	
   319	use super::SyscallReturn;
   320	use crate::{
   321	    fs::{
   322	        file_table::FileDesc,
   323	        fs_resolver::{FsPath, SplitPath, AT_FDCWD},
   324	    },
   325	    prelude::*,
   326	    syscall::constants::MAX_FILENAME_LEN,
   327	};
   328	
   329	pub fn sys_unlinkat(
   330	    dirfd: FileDesc,
   331	    path_addr: Vaddr,
   332	    flags: u32,
   333	    ctx: &Context,
   334	) -> Result<SyscallReturn> {
   335	    let flags =
   336	        UnlinkFlags::from_bits(flags).ok_or(Error::with_message(Errno::EINVAL, "invalid flags"))?;
   337	    if flags.contains(UnlinkFlags::AT_REMOVEDIR) {
   338	        return super::rmdir::sys_rmdirat(dirfd, path_addr, ctx);
   339	    }
   340	
   341	    let path_name = ctx.user_space().read_cstring(path_addr, MAX_FILENAME_LEN)?;
   342	    debug!("dirfd = {}, path = {:?}", dirfd, path_name);
   343	
   344	    let path_name = path_name.to_string_lossy();
   345	    let (dir_path, name) = {
   346	        let (parent_path_name, target_name) = path_name.split_dirname_and_filename()?;
   347	        let fs_path = FsPath::from_fd_and_path(dirfd, parent_path_name)?;
   348	        (
   349	            ctx.thread_local
   350	                .borrow_fs()
   351	                .resolver()
   352	                .read()
   353	                .lookup(&fs_path)?,
   354	            target_name,
   355	        )
   356	    };
   357	
   358	    dir_path.unlink(name)?;
   359	    Ok(SyscallReturn::Return(0))
   360	}

   746	        if name.len() > NAME_MAX {
   747	            return_errno!(Errno::ENAMETOOLONG);
   748	        }

   750	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "self is not dir");

   754	        if self_dir.contains_entry(name) {
   755	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EEXIST, "entry exists");
   756	        }

   770	            }
   771	        };

   774	        self_dir.append_entry(name, new_inode.clone());
   775	        drop(self_dir);

   778	        let mut inode_meta = self.metadata.lock();

  1104	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "self is not symlink");
  1105	        }

   170	    fn mappable(&self) -> Result<Mappable> {
   171	        let inode = self.path.inode();
   172	        if inode.page_cache().is_some() {
   173	            // If the inode has a page cache, it is a file-backed mapping and
   174	            // we directly return the corresponding inode.
   175	            Ok(Mappable::Inode(inode.clone()))
   176	        } else if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
   177	            // Otherwise, it is a special file (e.g. device file) and we should
   178	            // return the file-specific mappable object.
   179	            file_io.mappable()
   180	        } else {
   181	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "the file is not mappable");
   182	        }
   183	    }

   185	    fn test_range_lock(&self, lock: RangeLockItem) -> Result<RangeLockItem> {
   186	        let mut req_lock = lock.clone();
   187	        if let Some(extension) = self.path.inode().extension() {
   188	            if let Some(range_lock_list) = extension.get::<RangeLockList>() {
   189	                req_lock = range_lock_list.test_lock(lock);
   190	            } else {
   191	                // The range lock could be placed if there is no lock list
   192	                req_lock.set_type(RangeLockType::Unlock);
   193	            }
   194	        } else {
   195	            debug!("Inode extension is not supported, the lock could be placed");
   196	            // Some file systems may not support range lock like procfs and sysfs
   197	            // Returns Ok if extension is not supported.
   198	            req_lock.set_type(RangeLockType::Unlock);
   199	        }
   200	        Ok(req_lock)
   201	    }
   202	
   203	    fn set_range_lock(&self, lock: &RangeLockItem, is_nonblocking: bool) -> Result<()> {
   204	        if RangeLockType::Unlock == lock.type_() {
   205	            self.unlock_range_lock(lock);
   206	            return Ok(());
   207	        }
   208	
   209	        self.check_range_lock_with_access_mode(lock)?;
   210	        if let Some(extension) = self.path.inode().extension() {
   211	            let range_lock_list = match extension.get::<RangeLockList>() {
   212	                Some(list) => list,
   213	                None => extension.get_or_put_default::<RangeLockList>(),
   214	            };
   215	
   216	            range_lock_list.set_lock(lock, is_nonblocking)
   217	        } else {
   218	            debug!("Inode extension is not supported, let the lock could be acquired");
   219	            // Some file systems may not support range lock like procfs and sysfs
   220	            // Returns Ok if extension is not supported.
   221	            Ok(())
   222	        }
   223	    }
   224	
   225	    fn release_range_locks(&self) {
   226	        if self.path.inode().extension().is_none() {
   227	            return;
   228	        }
   229	
   230	        let range_lock = RangeLockItem::new(
   231	            RangeLockType::Unlock,
   232	            FileRange::new(0, OFFSET_MAX).unwrap(),
   233	        );
   234	        self.unlock_range_lock(&range_lock);
   235	    }
   236	
   237	    fn unlock_range_lock(&self, lock: &RangeLockItem) {
   238	        if let Some(extension) = self.path.inode().extension() {
   239	            if let Some(range_lock_list) = extension.get::<RangeLockList>() {
   240	                range_lock_list.unlock(lock);
   241	            }
   242	        }
   243	    }
   244	
   245	    fn check_range_lock_with_access_mode(&self, lock: &RangeLockItem) -> Result<()> {
   246	        match lock.type_() {
   247	            RangeLockType::ReadLock => {
   248	                if !self.access_mode().is_readable() {
   249	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EBADF, "file not readable");
   250	                }
   251	            }
   252	            RangeLockType::WriteLock => {
   253	                if !self.access_mode().is_writable() {
   254	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EBADF, "file not writable");
   255	                }
   256	            }
   257	            _ => (),
   258	        }
   259	        Ok(())
   260	    }

    99	    /// the result unresolved.
   100	    ///
   101	    /// An unresolved result may indicate either successful full-path resolution,
   102	    /// or resolution stopping at a parent path when the target doesn't exist
   103	    /// but its parent does.
   104	    ///
   105	    /// Symlinks are always followed.
   106	    pub fn lookup_unresolved(&self, fs_path: &FsPath) -> Result<LookupResult> {

   108	    }

   110	    /// Lookups the target path according to the `fs_path` and leaves
   111	    /// the result unresolved.
   112	    ///
   113	    /// An unresolved result may indicate either successful full-path resolution,
   114	    /// or resolution stopping at a parent path when the target doesn't exist
   115	    /// but its parent does.
   116	    ///
   117	    /// If the last component is a symlink, it will not be followed.
   118	    pub fn lookup_unresolved_no_follow(&self, fs_path: &FsPath) -> Result<LookupResult> {
   119	        self.lookup_inner(fs_path, false)
   120	    }
   121	
   122	    fn lookup_inner(&self, fs_path: &FsPath, follow_tail_link: bool) -> Result<LookupResult> {
   123	        let path = match fs_path.inner {
   124	            FsPathInner::Absolute(path) => {
   125	                self.lookup_from_parent(&self.root, path.trim_start_matches('/'), follow_tail_link)?
   126	            }
   127	            FsPathInner::CwdRelative(path) => {
   128	                self.lookup_from_parent(&self.cwd, path, follow_tail_link)?
   129	            }
   130	            FsPathInner::Cwd => LookupResult::Resolved(self.cwd.clone()),
   131	            FsPathInner::FdRelative(fd, path) => {
   132	                let task = Task::current().unwrap();
   133	                let mut file_table = task.as_thread_local().unwrap().borrow_file_table_mut();
   134	                let file = get_file_fast!(&mut file_table, fd);
   135	                self.lookup_from_parent(file.as_inode_or_err()?.path(), path, follow_tail_link)?
   136	            }
   137	            FsPathInner::Fd(fd) => {
   138	                let task = Task::current().unwrap();
   139	                let mut file_table = task.as_thread_local().unwrap().borrow_file_table_mut();
   140	                let file = get_file_fast!(&mut file_table, fd);
   141	                LookupResult::Resolved(file.as_inode_or_err()?.path().clone())
   142	            }
   143	        };
   144	
   145	        Ok(path)
   146	    }
   147	
   148	    /// Lookups the target path according to the parent directory path.
   149	    ///
   150	    /// The length of `path` cannot exceed `PATH_MAX`.
   151	    /// If `path` ends with `/`, then the returned inode must be a directory inode.
   152	    ///
   153	    /// While looking up the path, symbolic links will be followed for
   154	    /// at most `SYMLINKS_MAX` times.
   155	    ///
   156	    /// If `follow_tail_link` is true and the trailing component is a symlink,
   157	    /// it will be followed.
   158	    /// Symlinks in earlier components of the path will always be followed.
   159	    #[expect(clippy::redundant_closure)]
   160	    fn lookup_from_parent(
   161	        &self,
   162	        parent: &Path,
   163	        relative_path: &str,
   164	        follow_tail_link: bool,
   165	    ) -> Result<LookupResult> {
   166	        debug_assert!(!relative_path.starts_with('/'));
   167	
   168	        if relative_path.len() > PATH_MAX {
   169	            return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENAMETOOLONG, "the path is too long");
   170	        }
   171	        if relative_path.is_empty() {
   172	            return Ok(LookupResult::Resolved(parent.clone()));
   173	        }
   174	
   175	        // To handle symlinks
   176	        let mut link_path_opt = None;
   177	        let mut follows = 0;
   178	
   179	        // Initialize the first path and the relative path
   180	        let (mut current_path, mut relative_path) = (parent.clone(), relative_path);
   181	
   182	        while !relative_path.is_empty() {
   183	            let (next_name, path_remain, target_is_dir) =
   184	                if let Some((prefix, suffix)) = relative_path.split_once('/') {
   185	                    let suffix = suffix.trim_start_matches('/');
   186	                    (prefix, suffix, true)
   187	                } else {
   188	                    (relative_path, "", false)
   189	                };
   190	
   191	            // Iterate next path
   192	            let next_is_tail = path_remain.is_empty();
   193	            let next_path = match current_path.lookup(next_name) {
   194	                Ok(current_dir) => current_dir,
   195	                Err(e) => {
   196	                    if next_is_tail && e.error() == Errno::ENOENT {
   197	                        return Ok(LookupResult::AtParent(LookupParentResult::new(
   198	                            current_path,
   199	                            next_name.to_string(),
   200	                            target_is_dir,
   201	                        )));
   202	                    }
   203	                    return Err(e);
   204	                }
   205	            };
   206	
   207	            let next_type = next_path.type_();
   208	            // If next inode is a symlink, follow symlinks at most `SYMLINKS_MAX` times.
   209	            if next_type == InodeType::SymLink && (follow_tail_link || !next_is_tail) {
   210	                if follows >= SYMLINKS_MAX {
   211	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ELOOP, "there are too many symlinks");
   212	                }
   213	                let link_path_remain = {
   214	                    let mut tmp_link_path = next_path.inode().read_link()?;
   215	                    if tmp_link_path.is_empty() {
   216	                        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOENT, "the symlink path is empty");
   217	                    }
   218	                    if !path_remain.is_empty() {
   219	                        tmp_link_path += "/";
   220	                        tmp_link_path += path_remain;
   221	                    } else if target_is_dir {
   222	                        tmp_link_path += "/";
   223	                    }
   224	                    tmp_link_path
   225	                };
   226	
   227	                // Change the path and relative path according to symlink
   228	                if link_path_remain.starts_with('/') {
   229	                    current_path = self.root.clone();
   230	                }
   231	                let link_path = link_path_opt.get_or_insert_with(|| String::new());
   232	                link_path.clear();
   233	                link_path.push_str(link_path_remain.trim_start_matches('/'));
   234	                relative_path = link_path;
   235	                follows += 1;
   236	            } else {
   237	                // If path ends with `/`, the inode must be a directory
   238	                if target_is_dir && next_type != InodeType::Dir {
   239	                    return_errno_with_message!(Errno::ENOTDIR, "the inode is not a directory");
   240	                }
   241	                current_path = next_path;
   242	                relative_path = path_remain;
   243	            }
   244	        }
   245

   262	    fn set_flock(&self, lock: FlockItem, is_nonblocking: bool) -> Result<()> {
   263	        if let Some(extension) = self.path.inode().extension() {
   264	            let flock_list = match extension.get::<FlockList>() {
   265	                Some(list) => list,
   266	                None => extension.get_or_put_default::<FlockList>(),
   267	            };
   268	
   269	            flock_list.set_lock(lock, is_nonblocking)
   270	        } else {
   271	            debug!("Inode extension is not supported, let the lock could be acquired");
   272	            // Some file systems may not support flock like procfs and sysfs
   273	            // Returns Ok if extension is not supported.
   274	            Ok(())
   275	        }
   276	    }

   278	    fn unlock_flock<R>(&self, req_owner: &InodeHandle<R>) {
   279	        if let Some(extension) = self.path.inode().extension() {
   280	            if let Some(flock_list) = extension.get::<FlockList>() {
   281	                flock_list.unlock(req_owner);
   282	            }
   283	        }
   284	    }

   361	}

   363	pub fn do_seek_util(inode: &Arc<dyn Inode>, offset: &Mutex<usize>, pos: SeekFrom) -> Result<usize> {
   364	    let mut offset = offset.lock();
   365	    let new_offset: isize = match pos {
   366	        SeekFrom::Start(off /* as usize */) => {
   367	            if off > isize::MAX as usize {
   368	                return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "file offset is too large");
   369	            }
   370	            off as isize
   371	        }
   372	        SeekFrom::End(off /* as isize */) => {
   373	            let file_size = inode.size() as isize;
   374	            assert!(file_size >= 0);
   375	            file_size
   376	                .checked_add(off)
   377	                .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EOVERFLOW, "file offset overflow"))?
   378	        }
   379	        SeekFrom::Current(off /* as isize */) => (*offset as isize)
   380	            .checked_add(off)
   381	            .ok_or_else(|| Error::with_message(Errno::EOVERFLOW, "file offset overflow"))?,
   382	    };
   383	    if new_offset < 0 {
   384	        return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EINVAL, "file offset must not be negative");
   385	    }
   386	    // Invariant: 0 <= new_offset <= isize::MAX
   387	    let new_offset = new_offset as usize;
   388	    *offset = new_offset;
   389	    Ok(new_offset)
   390	}

    76	///
    77	/// The string is terminated with a null byte.
    78	#[repr(C)]
    79	#[derive(Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Pod)]
    80	pub struct FixedCStr<const N: usize>([u8; N]);
    81	
    82	impl<const N: usize> FixedCStr<N> {
    83	    pub fn len(&self) -> usize {
    84	        self.0.iter().position(|&b| b == 0).unwrap()
    85	    }
    86	
    87	    #[expect(dead_code)]
    88	    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool {
    89	        self.len() == 0
    90	    }

    92	    pub fn as_str(&self) -> Result<&str> {
    93	        Ok(alloc::str::from_utf8(self.as_bytes())?)
    94	    }
    95	
    96	    pub fn as_cstr(&self) -> Result<&CStr> {
    97	        Ok(CStr::from_bytes_with_nul(self.as_bytes_with_nul())?)
    98	    }
    99	
   100	    pub fn as_bytes(&self) -> &[u8] {
   101	        &self.0[0..self.len()]
   102	    }
   103	
   104	    pub fn as_bytes_with_nul(&self) -> &[u8] {
   105	        &self.0[0..=self.len()]
   106	    }
   107	}
   108	
   109	impl<'a, const N: usize> From<&'a [u8]> for FixedCStr<N> {
   110	    fn from(bytes: &'a [u8]) -> Self {
   111	        assert!(N > 0);
   112	
   113	        let mut inner = [0u8; N];
   114	        let len = {
   115	            let mut nul_byte_idx = match bytes.iter().position(|&b| b == 0) {
   116	                Some(idx) => idx,
   117	                None => bytes.len(),
   118	            };
   119	            if nul_byte_idx >= N {
   120	                nul_byte_idx = N - 1;
   121	            }
   122	            nul_byte_idx
   123	        };
   124	        inner[0..len].copy_from_slice(&bytes[0..len]);
   125	        Self(inner)
   126	    }
   127	}
   128

   130	    fn from(string: &'a str) -> Self {
   131	        let bytes = string.as_bytes();
   132	        Self::from(bytes)
   133	    }
   134	}

# For Maintainability

*Is the shape of the change sound, and will the next reader understand it without archaeology?*

This is the index of the **maintainability** guidelines.
Each subsection is its own page,
and each entry below links a stable `short-name` to its guideline,
with a one-line gist so a reader (or a review tool) can grasp the guideline before opening it.

## Index

**[Design](design.md)**
- [`single-responsibility`](design.md#single-responsibility): Each module, type, or function has one reason to change; keep functions small and one concept per file.
- [`dry`](design.md#dry): Give every piece of knowledge a single representation; eliminate duplication once a pattern recurs.
- [`information-hiding`](design.md#information-hiding): Hide details behind interfaces; expose only what consumers need.
- [`open-closed`](design.md#open-closed): Extend stable modules through existing interfaces; don't add extension points preemptively.
- [`least-surprise`](design.md#least-surprise): Names, types, and APIs behave as they suggest; prefer conventions users already know from Rust and Linux.
- [`coupling-cohesion`](design.md#coupling-cohesion): Keep inter-module connections small and visible; keep each module focused on one purpose.
- [`consistency`](design.md#consistency): Do similar things the same way; follow an existing convention rather than coining a competing one.
- [`rust-native`](design.md#rust-native): Learn from Linux's design, not its C idioms; write idiomatic Rust.

**[Process](process.md)**
- [`imperative-subject`](process.md#imperative-subject): Write each commit subject in the imperative mood, ≤72 chars, verb-first ("Fix", "Add", "Remove"); backtick identifiers.
- [`atomic-commits`](process.md#atomic-commits): One logical change per commit; don't mix unrelated changes.
- [`refactor-then-feature`](process.md#refactor-then-feature): Put preparatory refactoring in its own earlier commit(s), separate from the feature.
- [`focused-prs`](process.md#focused-prs): Keep a PR on a single topic; ensure CI passes before requesting review.

**[Naming](naming.md)**
- [`descriptive-names`](naming.md#descriptive-names): Names convey meaning at the point of use; avoid single letters and ambiguous abbreviations.
- [`accurate-names`](naming.md#accurate-names): Avoid names that mislead about meaning, behavior, or side effects.
- [`no-magic-number`](naming.md#no-magic-number): Give semantic names to numbers that encode rules, limits, units, masks, or external constants.
- [`encode-units`](naming.md#encode-units): When the type doesn't carry the unit, put it in the name (`timeout_ns`, `size_pages`).
- [`bool-names`](naming.md#bool-names): Name booleans as positive assertions (`is_`/`has_`/`can_`/…); avoid negation.
- [`error-message-format`](naming.md#error-message-format): Lowercase start (unless a proper noun), specific, Linux man-page style for syscall errors.

**[Layout](layout.md)**
- [`top-down-reading`](layout.md#top-down-reading): Order a file top-down: entry points and core flow first, detail below.
- [`logical-paragraphs`](layout.md#logical-paragraphs): Group related statements into blank-line-separated paragraphs, each one sub-step.

**[Comments](comments.md)**
- [`explain-why`](comments.md#explain-why): Comments explain intent (why), not what the code does; if you must explain "what", rewrite the code.
- [`design-decisions`](comments.md#design-decisions): Document non-obvious choices, with rationale and alternatives considered.
- [`cite-sources`](comments.md#cite-sources): Cite the source (POSIX, Linux man page, hardware manual, paper) for spec/algorithm behavior.

**[Rust-Specific](rust-specific/)**
- [Naming](rust-specific/naming.md)
    - [`camel-case-acronyms`](rust-specific/naming.md#camel-case-acronyms): Use Rust CamelCase with title-cased acronyms (`Nvme`, not `NVME`).
    - [`closure-fn-suffix`](rust-specific/naming.md#closure-fn-suffix): End a variable holding a closure or fn pointer with `_fn`.
- [Crates & Modules](rust-specific/crates-and-modules.md)
    - [`workspace-deps`](rust-specific/crates-and-modules.md#workspace-deps): Declare shared dependencies in `[workspace.dependencies]` and reference them with `.workspace = true`.
    - [`module-docs`](rust-specific/crates-and-modules.md#module-docs): Open a major module with a `//!` doc: purpose, key types, relation to neighbors.
    - [`narrow-visibility`](rust-specific/crates-and-modules.md#narrow-visibility): Start private; widen visibility only when an actual consumer requires it.
    - [`qualified-fn-imports`](rust-specific/crates-and-modules.md#qualified-fn-imports): Import the parent module and call free functions/statics through it, not by bare name.
- [Types & Traits](rust-specific/types-and-traits.md)
    - [`rust-type-invariants`](rust-specific/types-and-traits.md#rust-type-invariants): Use the type system (newtypes, enums, generics) to make illegal states unrepresentable.
    - [`enum-over-dyn`](rust-specific/types-and-traits.md#enum-over-dyn): For a closed set of variants, prefer an `enum` over `Box<dyn Trait>`.
    - [`getter-encapsulation`](rust-specific/types-and-traits.md#getter-encapsulation): Prefer a getter over a public field; it preserves naming freedom and room for invariants.
- [Functions & Methods](rust-specific/functions-and-methods.md)
    - [`no-bool-args`](rust-specific/functions-and-methods.md#no-bool-args): Avoid boolean parameters that select behavior; split the function or use a typed enum.
    - [`block-expressions`](rust-specific/functions-and-methods.md#block-expressions): Use a block expression to scope temporary state that only produces one value.
    - [`minimize-nesting`](rust-specific/functions-and-methods.md#minimize-nesting): Flatten nesting past ~3 levels with early returns, guard clauses, `let…else`, `?`, `continue`.
    - [`explain-variables`](rust-specific/functions-and-methods.md#explain-variables): Bind intermediate results of a complex expression to well-named variables.
- [Attributes & Macros](rust-specific/attributes-and-macros.md)
    - [`expect-dead-code`](rust-specific/attributes-and-macros.md#expect-dead-code): Allow `#[expect(dead_code)]` only for a planned, clear, simple future use.
    - [`alphabetical-attrs`](rust-specific/attributes-and-macros.md#alphabetical-attrs): Sort outer attributes alphabetically; place `#[derive(...)]` last with sorted traits.
    - [`narrow-lint-suppression`](rust-specific/attributes-and-macros.md#narrow-lint-suppression): Suppress a lint at the narrowest scope (item/method), not a whole type or module.
    - [`macros-as-last-resort`](rust-specific/attributes-and-macros.md#macros-as-last-resort): Prefer functions and generics; use a macro only when the type system can't express the need.
- [Comments](rust-specific/comments.md)
    - [`rfc1574-summary`](rust-specific/comments.md#rfc1574-summary): First doc line is one sentence — a third-person verb for functions, a noun phrase for types/modules.
    - [`comment-punctuation`](rust-specific/comments.md#comment-punctuation): End full-sentence comments with terminal punctuation.
    - [`backtick-identifiers`](rust-specific/comments.md#backtick-identifiers): Wrap identifiers in doc comments in backticks; prefer rustdoc links where possible.
    - [`no-impl-in-docs`](rust-specific/comments.md#no-impl-in-docs): Doc comments describe what an API does and how to use it, not its internal implementation.

No **path-specific** guidelines yet.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 3

> ```diff

> ```

`narrow-lint-suppression` (minor): The module-wide `#[expect(unused_variables)]` suppresses unused-variable diagnostics for every item in `inode_handle`, even though the known unused bindings are limited to the `FileIo` `read_at` and `write_at` TODO branches.

**Fix.** Move the expectation to the smallest relevant methods, or rewrite those branches so the `file_io` presence is tested without creating an unused binding.

### `kernel/src/fs/pipe.rs` line 72

`closure-fn-suffix` (minor): The local closures `read` and `write` are callable values but do not use the required `_fn` suffix, making them look like data rather than callbacks passed to `Endpoint`.

> ```diff
>     fn rmdir(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
>         if is_dot(name) {
> ```

Missing deletion authorization (major): `rmdir()` removes directories without checking write and search permission on the containing directory, allowing unauthorized directory removal.

**Fix.** Check containing-directory mutation permission and the directory ownership rules before removing the target.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 922

> ```diff
>     fn rename(&self, old_name: &str, target: &Arc<dyn Inode>, new_name: &str) -> Result<()> {
>         if is_dot_or_dotdot(old_name) {
> ```

Missing rename authorization (major): `rename()` performs no authorization on either parent directory. A caller with search access can move or replace entries in directories where it lacks write permission.

**Fix.** Require write and search permission on both source and destination parents, and enforce sticky-directory restrictions before mutating either directory.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 922

> ```diff
>     fn rename(&self, old_name: &str, target: &Arc<dyn Inode>, new_name: &str) -> Result<()> {
>         if is_dot_or_dotdot(old_name) {
> ```

Silent filename truncation (major): `rename()` does not validate `new_name` against `NAME_MAX`, but later stores it through `CStr256::from(...)` or `append_entry(...)`. A long `renameat()` name can disagree with the dentry cache and create colliding or unreachable entries.

**Fix.** Validate both `old_name` and `new_name` by byte length before taking directory locks or mutating the namespace.

## Correctness

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 76

> ```diff
>         if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND) {
>             *offset = self.path.size();
>         }
> 
>         let len = self.write_at(*offset, reader)?;
> ```

Append write race (major): Each `O_APPEND` handle reads `self.path.size()` and performs the write in separate operations. Two concurrent append writers can observe the same size and overwrite each other, losing data.

**Fix.** Provide an inode-level append-write operation that selects the end offset and writes while holding one shared serialization lock.

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 88

> ```diff
>         if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
>             todo!("support read_at for FileIo");
>         }
> 
>         if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_DIRECT) {
> ```

Reachable panic (critical): `pread64` and `pwrite64` on device-backed handles reach these `todo!` branches, so a normal positional I/O call on `/dev/zero` or another `FileIo` device panics the kernel instead of returning an error or performing the operation.

**Fix.** Add positional methods to `FileIo`, or return `ESPIPE`/`EOPNOTSUPP` here; do not leave user-reachable `todo!` paths.

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 424

> ```diff
>     if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND) {
>         // FIXME: It's allowed to `ftruncate` an append-only file on Linux.
>         return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPERM, "can not resize append-only file");
>     }
> ```

Incorrect error (major): `ftruncate()` on a descriptor carrying `O_APPEND` is rejected with `EPERM`, even though truncating an append-only descriptor is valid; this makes otherwise valid calls fail.

**Fix.** Remove this `O_APPEND` rejection from `do_resize_util`; keep append restrictions only for operations that change range placement.

### `kernel/src/fs/named_pipe.rs` line 35

> ```diff
> impl FileLike for NamedPipe {
>     fn read(&self, writer: &mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
>         self.reader.read(writer)
>     }
> 
>     fn write(&self, reader: &mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
>         self.writer.write(reader)
>     }
> ```

Ignored nonblocking mode (major): `NamedPipe::read` always calls the embedded `PipeReader`, whose status flags remain empty. An empty FIFO opened with `O_NONBLOCK` therefore enters the blocking path instead of returning `EAGAIN`; writes have the same problem.

**Fix.** Propagate `O_NONBLOCK` changes from the owning `InodeHandle` into both embedded endpoints, or make `NamedPipe` select the nonblocking operation itself.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 380

> ```diff
>             InodeType::NamedPipe => {
>                 warn!("named pipes don't support additional operation when opening");
>                 debug!("the named pipe is opened with {:?}", open_args);
>             }
> ```

Incorrect FIFO open behavior (major): Opening a named pipe performs no reader/writer rendezvous. `open()` returns immediately for blocking read-only or write-only FIFO opens, instead of waiting for the other side or returning `ENXIO` for nonblocking write-only opens.

**Fix.** Track FIFO open counts and apply the blocking/nonblocking open rules before constructing the `InodeHandle`.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 131

> ```diff
>     pub fn new_file(this: Weak<RamInode>) -> Self {
>         Self::File(PageCache::new(this).unwrap())
>     }
> ```

Reachable allocation panic (major): `PageCache::new()` returns an allocation error, but `Inner::new_file()` unwraps it. Creating a regular file under VMO or memory pressure can therefore panic the kernel instead of propagating the failure to `open()` or `create()`.

**Fix.** Make `Inner::new_file` and the inode constructors return `Result`, then propagate the `PageCache::new()` error through file creation.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 147

> ```diff
>     pub fn new_named_pipe() -> Self {
>         Self::NamedPipe(NamedPipe::new().unwrap())
>     }
> ```

Missing EOF after writer close (major): The pipe pair is owned by the inode, not by individual opens. Closing the last writer handle does not drop `PipeWriter`, so the reader's peer never reaches shutdown and a read after all writers close can block forever.

**Fix.** Move FIFO endpoint ownership to the open instances and maintain reader/writer counts so the underlying `PipeWriter` is dropped when the last writer closes.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 583

> ```diff
>                 let now = now();
>                 let mut inode_meta = self.metadata.lock();
>                 inode_meta.set_mtime(now);
>                 inode_meta.set_ctime(now);
>                 if should_expand_size {
>                     inode_meta.size = new_size;
>                     inode_meta.blocks = new_size_aligned / BLOCK_SIZE;
>                 }
> ```

Lost file size update (major): Concurrent writes compute `file_size` before any metadata lock is held and publish `size` afterward. For example, writes at offsets `0` and `100` can both observe size `0`, then the later metadata update can shrink the recorded size from `200` back to `100`.

**Fix.** Serialize page-cache resizing and metadata updates, or update the size under one lock using the maximum successfully written end offset.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 984

> ```diff
>                 check_replace_inode(&src_inode, &dst_inode)?;
>                 self_dir.remove_entry(dst_idx);
>                 self_dir.substitute_entry(src_idx, (CStr256::from(new_name), src_inode.clone()));
> ```

Stale link count (major): Replacing `new_name` removes `dst_inode` from the directory but never decrements its inode link count. An open descriptor for the replaced file or directory reports stale `nlinks`, and a replaced empty directory is not reduced to zero links.

**Fix.** When removing `dst_inode`, decrement its link count once for a non-directory and twice for an empty directory, and update its `ctime`.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 991

> ```diff
>                 let mut self_meta = self.metadata.lock();
>                 self_meta.dec_size();
>                 if is_dir {
>                     self_meta.dec_nlinks();
>                 }
>                 self_meta.set_mtime(now);
> ```

Incorrect directory link count (major): When a directory replaces an empty directory during same-directory rename, this decrements the parent link count even though the number of child directories is unchanged; the cross-directory replacement path likewise increments the destination count without removing the replaced directory.

**Fix.** For same-directory replacement, adjust the parent link count only by the difference between the removed destination and inserted source types. For cross-directory replacement, apply `-dst_inode.is_dir() + src_inode.is_dir()` to the relevant parent counts.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 1021

> ```diff
>             // Avoid renaming a directory to a subdirectory of itself
>             if Arc::ptr_eq(&src_inode, &target_inode_arc) {
>                 return_errno!(Errno::EINVAL);
>             }
> ```

Parent cycle (critical): The rename guard rejects only the case where the destination parent is exactly `src_inode`. Moving `/a` into `/a/b` passes this check, then makes `a` the parent of its own descendant; parent traversal can loop indefinitely and the VFS tree becomes cyclic.

**Fix.** Before mutating either directory, walk the destination parent's ancestor chain and reject the rename if it reaches `src_inode`, not only when the two inodes are equal.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 1142

> ```diff
>     fn poll(&self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<&mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
>         if !self.typ.is_device() {
>             return (IoEvents::IN | IoEvents::OUT) & mask;
>         }
> ```

Incorrect FIFO readiness (major): Every non-device inode, including `NamedPipe`, reports both `IN` and `OUT` unconditionally. An empty FIFO is reported readable and a full FIFO writable, so `poll`, `select`, and `epoll` can return false readiness.

**Fix.** Handle `InodeType::NamedPipe` separately and delegate readiness and poller registration to the underlying pipe endpoints.

## Security

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 348

> ```diff
>     pub fn mknod(&self, name: &str, mode: InodeMode, type_: MknodType) -> Result<Self> {
>         let inner = self.dentry.mknod(name, mode, type_)?;
> ```

Missing directory authorization (major): `mknod()` performs no permission check on the parent directory, allowing `mknodat()` to create FIFO or device entries in a directory the caller cannot modify.

**Fix.** Check parent write and search permissions before calling `self.dentry.mknod(...)`; also enforce the required device-creation capability for device nodes.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 354

> ```diff
>     pub fn link(&self, old: &Self, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
>         if !Arc::ptr_eq(&old.mount, &self.mount) {
> ```

Missing directory authorization (major): `link()` does not check write and search permission on the destination directory, so `linkat()` can add a hard link where the caller has only search access.

**Fix.** Require destination-directory `MAY_WRITE | MAY_EXEC` before calling `self.dentry.link(...)`, and apply the hard-link ownership restrictions at the same boundary.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 406

> ```diff
>         if inode_type.is_regular_file() && creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_TRUNC) {
>             self.resize(0)?;
>         }
>         InodeHandle::new(self.clone(), open_args.access_mode, open_args.status_flags)
> ```

Unauthorized truncation before access validation (major): `O_TRUNC` calls `self.resize(0)` before `InodeHandle::new()` validates the requested access. `openat(..., O_RDONLY | O_TRUNC)` can therefore truncate a regular file and then fail with `EACCES`, or succeed without write access.

**Fix.** Validate writable access and file permission before truncating, and reject `O_TRUNC` when `access_mode` is not writable.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 427

> ```diff
>             metadata: SpinLock::new(InodeMeta::new(mode, uid, gid)),
>             ino: weak_self.as_ptr() as u64,
>             typ: InodeType::File,
> ```

Kernel address disclosure (major): Detached memfd inodes use `weak_self.as_ptr()` as `ino`, and `fstat()` exposes that value through `st_ino`. This leaks a kernel heap address to userspace and can weaken kernel address randomization.

**Fix.** Allocate detached inode numbers from a non-address-based counter or another opaque identifier source; never expose kernel pointers as inode numbers.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 615

> ```diff
>     fn resize(&self, new_size: usize) -> Result<()> {
>         if self.typ != InodeType::File {
>             return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "not regular file");
> ```

Missing truncate authorization (major): `sys_truncate()` reaches `RamInode::resize()` through `Path::resize()`, but this method checks only inode type. A caller with path search access can truncate files regardless of ownership or write permission.

**Fix.** Perform the truncate authorization check at the syscall/path boundary, then call `resize()` only after validating ownership or the required capability and write policy.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 672

> ```diff
>     fn set_mode(&self, mode: InodeMode) -> Result<()> {
>         let mut inode_meta = self.metadata.lock();
>         inode_meta.mode = mode;
> ```

Missing chmod authorization (critical): `set_mode()` mutates permissions without checking that the caller owns the inode or has `CAP_FOWNER`. `chmod()` and `fchmod()` can therefore make arbitrary accessible files writable or executable.

**Fix.** Authorize mode changes using the caller's credentials and `CAP_FOWNER` before updating `inode_meta.mode`.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 683

> ```diff

> ```diff
>     fn set_owner(&self, uid: Uid) -> Result<()> {

Missing ownership authorization (critical): `set_owner()` accepts any `uid` without checking `CAP_CHOWN` or the ownership rules. An unprivileged caller can take ownership of a file and then alter its permissions or contents.

**Fix.** Require the appropriate ownership-change capability and validate the requested `uid` before changing the inode owner.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 694

>         inode_meta.gid = gid;
> ```

Missing group-change authorization (major): `set_group()` accepts any `gid` without checking the caller's ownership, supplementary groups, or `CAP_CHOWN`. An unprivileged caller can assign arbitrary group ownership.

**Fix.** Authorize group changes against the caller's credentials and require `CAP_CHOWN` when the normal ownership rules do not permit the requested group.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 825

> ```diff
>         if self_dir.contains_entry(name) {
>             return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EEXIST, "entry exist");
>         }
>         self_dir.append_entry(name, old.this.upgrade().unwrap());
> ```

Silent filename truncation (major): `link()` passes an unchecked user-supplied name to `CStr256::from(name)`, which silently truncates names longer than `NAME_MAX`. A `linkat()` name longer than `255` bytes can be stored under a different name and collide with another entry.

**Fix.** Reject names whose byte length exceeds `NAME_MAX` before inserting them; never rely on `CStr256` to clamp user input.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 842

> ```diff
>     fn unlink(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
>         if is_dot_or_dotdot(name) {
> ```

Missing deletion authorization (major): `unlink()` removes an entry without checking write and search permission on the containing directory. A process that can resolve the parent can delete files from a write-protected directory.

**Fix.** Authorize deletion against the containing directory before removing the entry, including sticky-directory ownership rules where applicable.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 874

> ```diff
>     fn rmdir(&self, name: &str) -> Result<()> {
>         if is_dot(name) {
> ```

Missing deletion authorization (major): `rmdir()` removes directories without checking write and search permission on the containing directory, allowing unauthorized directory removal.

**Fix.** Check containing-directory mutation permission and the directory ownership rules before removing the target.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 922

> ```diff
>     fn rename(&self, old_name: &str, target: &Arc<dyn Inode>, new_name: &str) -> Result<()> {
>         if is_dot_or_dotdot(old_name) {
> ```

Missing rename authorization (major): `rename()` performs no authorization on either parent directory. A caller with search access can move or replace entries in directories where it lacks write permission.

**Fix.** Require write and search permission on both source and destination parents, and enforce sticky-directory restrictions before mutating either directory.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 922

> ```diff
>     fn rename(&self, old_name: &str, target: &Arc<dyn Inode>, new_name: &str) -> Result<()> {
>         if is_dot_or_dotdot(old_name) {
> ```

Silent filename truncation (major): `rename()` does not validate `new_name` against `NAME_MAX`, but later stores it through `CStr256::from(...)` or `append_entry(...)`. A long `renameat()` name can disagree with the dentry cache and create colliding or unreachable entries.

**Fix.** Validate both `old_name` and `new_name` by byte length before taking directory locks or mutating the namespace.

 # Summary
 
 <!-- SUMMARY -->
+The reviewed filesystem code provides the core path, inode, RAMFS, and pipe plumbing, and it already has useful defensive checks for path component length, cross-filesystem operations, invalid dot entries, and non-empty directory replacement. The main risks are correctness and authorization gaps at the VFS boundary: FIFO lifecycle and open semantics are incomplete, concurrent file writes and renames can corrupt metadata, and several mutating operations change RAMFS state without checking caller credentials.
+
+The highest-severity issues are the user-reachable `todo!` panic in positional device I/O, the rename path's ability to create a parent cycle, and unrestricted `chmod`/`chown`-style metadata changes. Major issues include missing directory write checks for create/link/delete/rename, truncating before access validation, incorrect FIFO blocking/nonblocking/readiness behavior, and stale or racy inode metadata. The maintainability findings are comparatively minor and concern module documentation, lint scope, visibility, and closure naming.
+
+The fixes should be organized around shared authorization helpers for directory mutation and inode metadata changes, an inode-level serialization primitive for append and size updates, and per-open FIFO endpoint state with correct reader/writer counts. Add regression tests for each syscall-visible permission failure, FIFO open/close/readiness transition, concurrent append/write behavior, and rename replacement/cycle cases.

129:### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 76
139:Append write race (major): Each `O_APPEND` handle reads `self.path.size()` and performs the write in separate operations. Two concurrent append writers can observe the same size and overwrite each other, losing data.
143:### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 88
153:Reachable panic (critical): `pread64` and `pwrite64` on device-backed handles reach these `todo!` branches, so a normal positional I/O call on `/dev/zero` or another `FileIo` device panics the kernel instead of returning an error or performing the operation.
157:### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 424
166:Incorrect error (major): `ftruncate()` on a descriptor carrying `O_APPEND` is rejected with `EPERM`, even though truncating an append-only descriptor is valid; this makes otherwise valid calls fail.
170:### `kernel/src/fs/named_pipe.rs` line 35
183:Ignored nonblocking mode (major): `NamedPipe::read` always calls the embedded `PipeReader`, whose status flags remain empty. An empty FIFO opened with `O_NONBLOCK` therefore enters the blocking path instead of returning `EAGAIN`; writes have the same problem.
187:### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 380
196:Incorrect FIFO open behavior (major): Opening a named pipe performs no reader/writer rendezvous. `open()` returns immediately for blocking read-only or write-only FIFO opens, instead of waiting for the other side or returning `ENXIO` for nonblocking write-only opens.
200:### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 131
208:Reachable allocation panic (major): `PageCache::new()` returns an allocation error, but `Inner::new_file()` unwraps it. Creating a regular file under VMO or memory pressure can therefore panic the kernel instead of propagating the failure to `open()` or `create()`.
212:### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 147
220:Missing EOF after writer close (major): The pipe pair is owned by the inode, not by individual opens. Closing the last writer handle does not drop `PipeWriter`, so the reader's peer never reaches shutdown and a read after all writers close can block forever.
224:### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 583
237:Lost file size update (major): Concurrent writes compute `file_size` before any metadata lock is held and publish `size` afterward. For example, writes at offsets `0` and `100` can both observe size `0`, then the later metadata update can shrink the recorded size from `200` back to `100`.
241:### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 984
249:Stale link count (major): Replacing `new_name` removes `dst_inode` from the directory but never decrements its inode link count. An open descriptor for the replaced file or directory reports stale `nlinks`, and a replaced empty directory is not reduced to zero links.
253:### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 991
264:Incorrect directory link count (major): When a directory replaces an empty directory during same-directory rename, this decrements the parent link count even though the number of child directories is unchanged; the cross-directory replacement path likewise increments the destination count without removing the replaced directory.
268:### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 1021
277:Parent cycle (critical): The rename guard rejects only the case where the destination parent is exactly `src_inode`. Moving `/a` into `/a/b` passes this check, then makes `a` the parent of its own descendant; parent traversal can loop indefinitely and the VFS tree becomes cyclic.
281:### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 1142
290:Incorrect FIFO readiness (major): Every non-device inode, including `NamedPipe`, reports both `IN` and `OUT` unconditionally. An empty FIFO is reported readable and a full FIFO writable, so `poll`, `select`, and `epoll` can return false readiness.

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Parse an aster-code-review review file into GitHub review pieces.

Reads environment variables and prints JSON to stdout:
  REVIEW_FILE  path to the review file (required)
  DIFF_FILE    path to a file holding the PR's unified diff (preferred). A large diff
               passed inline via DIFF overflows the exec arg list, so callers write it
               to a file and pass the path here.
  DIFF         the PR's unified diff inline (optional fallback, used by the tests).
               Comments whose line is not on the RIGHT side of the diff are moved to
               "dropped" (GitHub silently discards inline comments off the diff).
               An empty/absent diff drops nothing.

Output: {"summary": <str>, "comments": [<postable>], "dropped": [<off-diff>],
         "dropped_section": <str>}
where each comment is {"path", "body", and either "line"/"side"
(+ "start_line"/"start_side" for a range) or "subject_type": "file"}.
"dropped_section" is a ready-to-append Markdown rendering of "dropped" (empty when
nothing was dropped): the poster puts it below the summary so off-diff findings,
which GitHub cannot inline, are surfaced instead of silently lost.

Kept model-free and importable so tests/test_post_reviews.sh can exercise it
directly, without gh or the network.
"""
import re, json, os, sys


def render_dropped(dropped):
    """Render off-diff findings as a Markdown section for the review body.

    GitHub silently discards an inline comment whose line is not on the diff, so
    these would be lost. We surface them below the summary instead, hedged: a
    dropped finding is usually on a line this PR did not change (most likely a
    pre-existing issue) or is about a commit message. Returns "" when nothing was
    dropped. Location matches the inline form: `path` line N, or the bare `path`
    (a filename, or a "commit <sha> message" locus) when there is no line.
    """
    if not dropped:
        return ""
    out = ["---", "",
           "### Findings not attachable to this PR's diff", "",
           "> [!NOTE]",
           "> These couldn't be pinned to a changed line",
           "> — usually they sit on lines this PR didn't change (likely pre-existing issues),",
           "> or concern a commit message.",
           "> Listed here so they aren't lost.", ""]
    for c in dropped:
        if not c.get("line"):
            loc = "`%s`" % c["path"]                                  # file-level / commit message
        elif c.get("start_line") and c["start_line"] != c["line"]:
            loc = "`%s` lines %s-%s" % (c["path"], c["start_line"], c["line"])
        else:
            loc = "`%s` line %s" % (c["path"], c["line"])
        out += ["**%s** — %s" % (loc, c["body"]), ""]
    return "\n".join(out).rstrip()


def parse(text, diff):
    # strip YAML frontmatter (first --- ... --- pair)
    lines = text.split("\n")
    if lines and lines[0].strip() == "---":
        for i in range(1, len(lines)):
            if lines[i].strip() == "---":
                text = "\n".join(lines[i + 1:]); break

    # review body = `# Summary` .. first `## ` (persona); keep consolidated-fix blockquotes
    summary = ""
    m = re.search(r'^# Summary\s*$', text, re.M)
    if m:
        rest = text[m.end():]
        m2 = re.search(r'^## ', rest, re.M)
        summary = (rest[:m2.start()] if m2 else rest).strip()
        summary = re.sub(r'\n*-{3,}\s*$', '', summary).strip()   # drop a trailing --- rule

    # comments: each `### `path` line N` (or `lines N-M`, or bare path = file-level)
    heading = re.compile(r'^###\s+`([^`]+)`(?:\s+lines?\s+(\d+)(?:-(\d+))?)?\s*$', re.M)
    hits = list(heading.finditer(text))
    comments = []
    for i, mm in enumerate(hits):
        path, ls, le = mm.group(1), mm.group(2), mm.group(3)
        seg = text[mm.end(): hits[i + 1].start() if i + 1 < len(hits) else len(text)]
        kept = []
        for ln in seg.split("\n"):
            s = ln.strip()
            if s.startswith("##"):       # reached the next persona section
                break
            if s.startswith(">"):         # the quoted diff block — GitHub shows the diff already
                continue
            kept.append(ln)
        body = "\n".join(kept).strip()
        if not body:
            continue
        c = {"path": path, "body": body}
        if ls:
            c["line"] = int(le) if le else int(ls); c["side"] = "RIGHT"
            if le and le != ls:
                c["start_line"] = int(ls); c["start_side"] = "RIGHT"
        else:
            c["subject_type"] = "file"
        comments.append(c)

    # RIGHT-side line numbers present in the PR diff (added + context); GitHub drops the rest.
    # File boundaries are keyed off `diff --git`, and lines are counted ONLY inside a hunk, so
    # that (a) an added line whose own text starts with `+++ `/`--- ` is treated as content, not
    # a file header, and (b) the inter-file `diff --git`/`index` lines are not miscounted as
    # context for the previous file.
    postable, cur, newno, in_hunk = {}, None, 0, False
    for ln in (diff or "").split("\n"):
        if ln.startswith("diff --git "):
            cur, in_hunk = None, False       # leaving the previous file; next `+++ b/` sets cur
        elif ln.startswith("@@"):
            m = re.search(r'\+(\d+)', ln); newno = int(m.group(1)) if m else 0
            in_hunk = True
        elif not in_hunk and ln.startswith("+++ b/"):
            cur = ln[6:]; postable.setdefault(cur, set())
        elif not in_hunk and ln.startswith("+++ "):
            cur = None                       # e.g. `+++ /dev/null` (deleted file: no RIGHT side)
        elif in_hunk and cur is not None:
            if ln.startswith("+"):
                postable[cur].add(newno); newno += 1
            elif ln.startswith("-"):
                pass
            elif not ln.startswith("\\"):    # context line (incl. blank); ignore "\ No newline"
                postable[cur].add(newno); newno += 1

    # with no diff, nothing is dropped (postable empty -> treat all as kept)
    have_diff = bool(postable)
    kept_c, dropped = [], []
    for c in comments:
        if not have_diff:
            kept_c.append(c)
        elif c.get("subject_type") == "file":
            (kept_c if c["path"] in postable else dropped).append(c)
        elif c["path"] in postable and c["line"] in postable[c["path"]]:
            kept_c.append(c)
        else:
            dropped.append(c)
    return {"summary": summary, "comments": kept_c, "dropped": dropped,
            "dropped_section": render_dropped(dropped)}


if __name__ == "__main__":
    rf = os.environ.get("REVIEW_FILE")
    if not rf:
        sys.stderr.write("parse_review.py: REVIEW_FILE is required\n"); sys.exit(2)
    # Prefer DIFF_FILE (a path)
    # — a large PR diff overflows the exec arg list if passed inline via DIFF.
    # DIFF (inline) remains for the model-free tests.
    df = os.environ.get("DIFF_FILE")
    diff = open(df).read() if df else os.environ.get("DIFF", "")
    print(json.dumps(parse(open(rf).read(), diff)))

===== EXPECTED DEFECTS =====
# Expected defects

1. location: kernel/src/fs/named_pipe.rs lines 14-45 (persona: development, grounding: FIFO open must block until the peer endpoint is present, severity: major)
   defect: FIFO open currently returns in blocking mode even when the peer endpoint is absent. A blocking open(O_RDONLY) should wait until a writer opens the FIFO, and a blocking open(O_WRONLY) should wait until a reader opens it.
   MATCH IF: A valid review must say that FIFO open semantics are wrong because open returns before both read and write ends are present in blocking mode; it should request blocking in open based on access mode and peer presence.

2. location: kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs lines 552-600 (persona: development, grounding: named-pipe read/write ignore the fd's O_NONBLOCK flag, severity: major)
   defect: RamFS dispatches NamedPipe read/write directly to the shared NamedPipe object. NamedPipe::read and NamedPipe::write take no StatusFlags, and InodeHandle::read/write do not pass their per-fd status_flags into FileLike/FileIo. As a result, read/write behavior is determined by the shared pipe endpoints, not by the file descriptor that issued the operation, so O_NONBLOCK on a FIFO fd is not reliably honored.
   MATCH IF: A valid review must identify that named-pipe read/write need access to the caller fd's status_flags; otherwise O_NONBLOCK cannot control whether the operation returns EAGAIN or blocks.

3. location: kernel/src/fs/pipe.rs lines 50-123,158-234 (persona: development, grounding: status_flags must be per file descriptor, not shared by the pipe object, severity: major)
   defect: PipeReader and PipeWriter each store status_flags inside the shared pipe endpoint object. A FIFO inode has one shared NamedPipe with shared reader and writer endpoints, while each open fd has its own InodeHandle status_flags. If two descriptors open the same FIFO with different flags, such as one blocking reader and one nonblocking reader, storing the flag on the shared endpoint cannot represent both descriptors correctly.
   MATCH IF: A valid review must state that status_flags for a named pipe must be per-fd; a shared NamedPipe/PipeReader/PipeWriter flag makes descriptors interfere with each other and cannot support simultaneous blocking and nonblocking opens.

# Summary

<!-- SUMMARY -->
The reviewed filesystem code provides the core path, inode, RAMFS, and pipe plumbing, and it already has useful defensive checks for path component length, cross-filesystem operations, invalid dot entries, and non-empty directory replacement. The main risks are correctness and authorization gaps at the VFS boundary: FIFO lifecycle and open semantics are incomplete, concurrent file writes and renames can corrupt metadata, and several mutating operations change RAMFS state without checking caller credentials.

The highest-severity issues are the user-reachable `todo!` panic in positional device I/O, the rename path's ability to create a parent cycle, and unrestricted `chmod`/`chown`-style metadata changes. Major issues include missing directory write checks for create/link/delete/rename, truncating before access validation, incorrect FIFO blocking/nonblocking/readiness behavior, and stale or racy inode metadata. The maintainability findings are comparatively minor and concern module documentation, lint scope, visibility, and closure naming.

The fixes should be organized around shared authorization helpers for directory mutation and inode metadata changes, an inode-level serialization primitive for append and size updates, and per-open FIFO endpoint state with correct reader/writer counts. Add regression tests for each syscall-visible permission failure, FIFO open/close/readiness transition, concurrent append/write behavior, and rename replacement/cycle cases.

## Retracted by verification

- `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 49: the missing `MAY_EXEC` check in `new_fs_child()` is redundant for the reported syscall paths because unresolved path lookup already traverses the parent through `Path::lookup()`, which checks directory search permission.

## Maintainability

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 3

> ```diff
> #![expect(unused_variables)]
> ```

`narrow-lint-suppression` (minor): The module-wide `#[expect(unused_variables)]` suppresses unused-variable diagnostics for every item in `inode_handle`, even though the known unused bindings are limited to the `FileIo` `read_at` and `write_at` TODO branches.

**Fix.** Move the expectation to the smallest relevant methods, or rewrite those branches so the `file_io` presence is tested without creating an unused binding.

### `kernel/src/fs/named_pipe.rs` line 3

> ```diff
> use super::{
> ```

`module-docs` (minor): The public `named_pipe` module has no `//!` overview explaining that `NamedPipe` composes the reader and writer endpoints from `pipe`.

**Fix.** Add module-level documentation before the imports describing `NamedPipe` and its relationship to the underlying pipe endpoints.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 3

> ```diff
> //! Form file paths within and across FSes with dentries and mount points.
> ```

`rfc1574-summary` (minor): The module summary starts with the imperative verb `Form`, while module documentation should use a concise noun phrase.

**Fix.** Rewrite the summary as a noun phrase, for example `//! File paths within and across file systems, represented by [`Path`] and resolved through mounts and dentries.`

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 72

> ```

`rfc1574-summary` (minor): The `lookup` method's summary uses `Lookups`, which is not a third-person present-tense verb and does not read as a valid API summary.

**Fix.** Change the summary to `/// Looks up the target [`Path`] given the name.`

### `kernel/src/fs/pipe.rs` line 3

> ```diff

> ```

`module-docs` (minor): The public `pipe` module has no `//!` overview describing `PipeReader`, `PipeWriter`, or how the endpoints relate to the ring buffer and `Endpoint` state.

**Fix.** Add module-level documentation before the imports explaining the pipe abstraction, its exposed types, and its relationship to `RingBuffer` and `Endpoint`.

### `kernel/src/fs/pipe.rs` line 72

>     let mut consumer = self.consumer.lock();
>     consumer.read_fallible(writer)
> };
>

> ```

`closure-fn-suffix` (minor): The local closures `read` and `write` are callable values but do not use the required `_fn` suffix, making them look like data rather than callbacks passed to `Endpoint`.

**Fix.** Rename them to `read_fn` and `write_fn`, and pass those names to `read_with` and `write_with`.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 126

`narrow-visibility` (minor): The constructors on the private `Inner` enum are declared `pub`, although all callers are inside `ramfs::fs`; this exposes no useful external API and widens the implementation surface.

**Fix.** Make `Inner::new_dir`, `Inner::new_file`, `Inner::new_symlink`, `Inner::new_device`, `Inner::new_socket`, and `Inner::new_named_pipe` private.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 201

`narrow-visibility` (minor): All `InodeMeta` constructors and mutators are declared `pub` even though `InodeMeta` is private and every use is within `ramfs::fs`, unnecessarily exposing implementation details.

**Fix.** Change the `InodeMeta` methods to private `fn` methods; widen visibility only if a real caller outside this implementation is added.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 352
