## Correctness

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 76

> ```diff
>         if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND) {
>             *offset = self.path.size();
>         }
> 
>         let len = self.write_at(*offset, reader)?;
> ```

Append write race (major): Each `O_APPEND` handle reads `self.path.size()` and performs the write in separate operations. Two concurrent append writers can observe the same size and overwrite each other, losing data.

**Fix.** Provide an inode-level append-write operation that selects the end offset and writes while holding one shared serialization lock.

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 88

> ```diff
>         if let Some(ref file_io) = self.file_io {
>             todo!("support read_at for FileIo");
>         }
> 
>         if self.status_flags().contains(StatusFlags::O_DIRECT) {
> ```

Reachable panic (critical): `pread64` and `pwrite64` on device-backed handles reach these `todo!` branches, so a normal positional I/O call on `/dev/zero` or another `FileIo` device panics the kernel instead of returning an error or performing the operation.

**Fix.** Add positional methods to `FileIo`, or return `ESPIPE`/`EOPNOTSUPP` here; do not leave user-reachable `todo!` paths.

### `kernel/src/fs/inode_handle/mod.rs` line 424

> ```diff
>     if status_flags.contains(StatusFlags::O_APPEND) {
>         // FIXME: It's allowed to `ftruncate` an append-only file on Linux.
>         return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EPERM, "can not resize append-only file");
>     }
> ```

Incorrect error (major): `ftruncate()` on a descriptor carrying `O_APPEND` is rejected with `EPERM`, even though truncating an append-only descriptor is valid; this makes otherwise valid calls fail.

**Fix.** Remove this `O_APPEND` rejection from `do_resize_util`; keep append restrictions only for operations that change range placement.

### `kernel/src/fs/named_pipe.rs` line 35

> ```diff
> impl FileLike for NamedPipe {
>     fn read({prompt}self, writer: {prompt}mut VmWriter) -> Result<usize> {
>         self.reader.read(writer)
>     }
> 
>     fn write({prompt}self, reader: {prompt}mut VmReader) -> Result<usize> {
>         self.writer.write(reader)
>     }
> ```

Ignored nonblocking mode (major): `NamedPipe::read` always calls the embedded `PipeReader`, whose status flags remain empty. An empty FIFO opened with `O_NONBLOCK` therefore enters the blocking path instead of returning `EAGAIN`; writes have the same problem.

**Fix.** Propagate `O_NONBLOCK` changes from the owning `InodeHandle` into both embedded endpoints, or make `NamedPipe` select the nonblocking operation itself.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 380

> ```diff
>             InodeType::NamedPipe => {
>                 warn!("named pipes don't support additional operation when opening");
>                 debug!("the named pipe is opened with {:?}", open_args);
>             }
> ```

Incorrect FIFO open behavior (major): Opening a named pipe performs no reader/writer rendezvous. `open()` returns immediately for blocking read-only or write-only FIFO opens, instead of waiting for the other side or returning `ENXIO` for nonblocking write-only opens.

**Fix.** Track FIFO open counts and apply the blocking/nonblocking open rules before constructing the `InodeHandle`.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 131

> ```diff
>     pub fn new_file(this: Weak<RamInode>) -> Self {
>         Self::File(PageCache::new(this).unwrap())
>     }
> ```

Reachable allocation panic (major): `PageCache::new()` returns an allocation error, but `Inner::new_file()` unwraps it. Creating a regular file under VMO or memory pressure can therefore panic the kernel instead of propagating the failure to `open()` or `create()`.

**Fix.** Make `Inner::new_file` and the inode constructors return `Result`, then propagate the `PageCache::new()` error through file creation.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 147

> ```diff
>     pub fn new_named_pipe() -> Self {
>         Self::NamedPipe(NamedPipe::new().unwrap())
>     }
> ```

Missing EOF after writer close (major): The pipe pair is owned by the inode, not by individual opens. Closing the last writer handle does not drop `PipeWriter`, so the reader's peer never reaches shutdown and a read after all writers close can block forever.

**Fix.** Move FIFO endpoint ownership to the open instances and maintain reader/writer counts so the underlying `PipeWriter` is dropped when the last writer closes.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 583

> ```diff
>                 let now = now();
>                 let mut inode_meta = self.metadata.lock();
>                 inode_meta.set_mtime(now);
>                 inode_meta.set_ctime(now);
>                 if should_expand_size {
>                     inode_meta.size = new_size;
>                     inode_meta.blocks = new_size_aligned / BLOCK_SIZE;
>                 }
> ```

Lost file size update (major): Concurrent writes compute `file_size` before any metadata lock is held and publish `size` afterward. For example, writes at offsets `0` and `100` can both observe size `0`, then the later metadata update can shrink the recorded size from `200` back to `100`.

**Fix.** Serialize page-cache resizing and metadata updates, or update the size under one lock using the maximum successfully written end offset.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 984

> ```diff
>                 check_replace_inode({prompt}src_inode, {prompt}dst_inode)?;
>                 self_dir.remove_entry(dst_idx);
>                 self_dir.substitute_entry(src_idx, (CStr256::from(new_name), src_inode.clone()));
> ```

Stale link count (major): Replacing `new_name` removes `dst_inode` from the directory but never decrements its inode link count. An open descriptor for the replaced file or directory reports stale `nlinks`, and a replaced empty directory is not reduced to zero links.

**Fix.** When removing `dst_inode`, decrement its link count once for a non-directory and twice for an empty directory, and update its `ctime`.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 991

> ```diff
>                 let mut self_meta = self.metadata.lock();
>                 self_meta.dec_size();
>                 if is_dir {
>                     self_meta.dec_nlinks();
>                 }
>                 self_meta.set_mtime(now);
> ```

Incorrect directory link count (major): When a directory replaces an empty directory during same-directory rename, this decrements the parent link count even though the number of child directories is unchanged; the cross-directory replacement path likewise increments the destination count without removing the replaced directory.

**Fix.** For same-directory replacement, adjust the parent link count only by the difference between the removed destination and inserted source types. For cross-directory replacement, apply `-dst_inode.is_dir() + src_inode.is_dir()` to the relevant parent counts.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 1021

> ```diff
>             // Avoid renaming a directory to a subdirectory of itself
>             if Arc::ptr_eq({prompt}src_inode, {prompt}target_inode_arc) {
>                 return_errno!(Errno::EINVAL);
>             }
> ```

Parent cycle (critical): The rename guard rejects only the case where the destination parent is exactly `src_inode`. Moving `/a` into `/a/b` passes this check, then makes `a` the parent of its own descendant; parent traversal can loop indefinitely and the VFS tree becomes cyclic.

**Fix.** Before mutating either directory, walk the destination parent's ancestor chain and reject the rename if it reaches `src_inode`, not only when the two inodes are equal.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 1142

> ```diff
>     fn poll({prompt}self, mask: IoEvents, poller: Option<{prompt}mut PollHandle>) -> IoEvents {
>         if !self.typ.is_device() {
>             return (IoEvents::IN | IoEvents::OUT) {prompt} mask;
>         }
> ```

Incorrect FIFO readiness (major): Every non-device inode, including `NamedPipe`, reports both `IN` and `OUT` unconditionally. An empty FIFO is reported readable and a full FIFO writable, so `poll`, `select`, and `epoll` can return false readiness.

**Fix.** Handle `InodeType::NamedPipe` separately and delegate readiness and poller registration to the underlying pipe endpoints.

## Security

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 348

> ```diff
>     pub fn mknod({prompt}self, name: {prompt}str, mode: InodeMode, type_: MknodType) -> Result<Self> {
>         let inner = self.dentry.mknod(name, mode, type_)?;
> ```

Missing directory authorization (major): `mknod()` performs no permission check on the parent directory, allowing `mknodat()` to create FIFO or device entries in a directory the caller cannot modify.

**Fix.** Check parent write and search permissions before calling `self.dentry.mknod(...)`; also enforce the required device-creation capability for device nodes.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 354

> ```diff
>     pub fn link({prompt}self, old: {prompt}Self, name: {prompt}str) -> Result<()> {
>         if !Arc::ptr_eq({prompt}old.mount, {prompt}self.mount) {
> ```

Missing directory authorization (major): `link()` does not check write and search permission on the destination directory, so `linkat()` can add a hard link where the caller has only search access.

**Fix.** Require destination-directory `MAY_WRITE | MAY_EXEC` before calling `self.dentry.link(...)`, and apply the hard-link ownership restrictions at the same boundary.

### `kernel/src/fs/path/mod.rs` line 406

> ```diff
>         if inode_type.is_regular_file() {prompt}{prompt} creation_flags.contains(CreationFlags::O_TRUNC) {
>             self.resize(0)?;
>         }
>         InodeHandle::new(self.clone(), open_args.access_mode, open_args.status_flags)
> ```

Unauthorized truncation before access validation (major): `O_TRUNC` calls `self.resize(0)` before `InodeHandle::new()` validates the requested access. `openat(..., O_RDONLY | O_TRUNC)` can therefore truncate a regular file and then fail with `EACCES`, or succeed without write access.

**Fix.** Validate writable access and file permission before truncating, and reject `O_TRUNC` when `access_mode` is not writable.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 427

> ```diff
>             metadata: SpinLock::new(InodeMeta::new(mode, uid, gid)),
>             ino: weak_self.as_ptr() as u64,
>             typ: InodeType::File,
> ```

Kernel address disclosure (major): Detached memfd inodes use `weak_self.as_ptr()` as `ino`, and `fstat()` exposes that value through `st_ino`. This leaks a kernel heap address to userspace and can weaken kernel address randomization.

**Fix.** Allocate detached inode numbers from a non-address-based counter or another opaque identifier source; never expose kernel pointers as inode numbers.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 615

> ```diff
>     fn resize({prompt}self, new_size: usize) -> Result<()> {
>         if self.typ != InodeType::File {
>             return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EISDIR, "not regular file");
> ```

Missing truncate authorization (major): `sys_truncate()` reaches `RamInode::resize()` through `Path::resize()`, but this method checks only inode type. A caller with path search access can truncate files regardless of ownership or write permission.

**Fix.** Perform the truncate authorization check at the syscall/path boundary, then call `resize()` only after validating ownership or the required capability and write policy.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 672

> ```diff
>     fn set_mode({prompt}self, mode: InodeMode) -> Result<()> {
>         let mut inode_meta = self.metadata.lock();
>         inode_meta.mode = mode;
> ```

Missing chmod authorization (critical): `set_mode()` mutates permissions without checking that the caller owns the inode or has `CAP_FOWNER`. `chmod()` and `fchmod()` can therefore make arbitrary accessible files writable or executable.

**Fix.** Authorize mode changes using the caller's credentials and `CAP_FOWNER` before updating `inode_meta.mode`.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 683

> ```diff
>     fn set_owner({prompt}self, uid: Uid) -> Result<()> {
>         let mut inode_meta = self.metadata.lock();
>         inode_meta.uid = uid;
> ```

Missing ownership authorization (critical): `set_owner()` accepts any `uid` without checking `CAP_CHOWN` or the ownership rules. An unprivileged caller can take ownership of a file and then alter its permissions or contents.

**Fix.** Require the appropriate ownership-change capability and validate the requested `uid` before changing the inode owner.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 694

> ```diff
>     fn set_group({prompt}self, gid: Gid) -> Result<()> {
>         let mut inode_meta = self.metadata.lock();
>         inode_meta.gid = gid;
> ```

Missing group-change authorization (major): `set_group()` accepts any `gid` without checking the caller's ownership, supplementary groups, or `CAP_CHOWN`. An unprivileged caller can assign arbitrary group ownership.

**Fix.** Authorize group changes against the caller's credentials and require `CAP_CHOWN` when the normal ownership rules do not permit the requested group.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 825

> ```diff
>         if self_dir.contains_entry(name) {
>             return_errno_with_message!(Errno::EEXIST, "entry exist");
>         }
>         self_dir.append_entry(name, old.this.upgrade().unwrap());
> ```

Silent filename truncation (major): `link()` passes an unchecked user-supplied name to `CStr256::from(name)`, which silently truncates names longer than `NAME_MAX`. A `linkat()` name longer than `255` bytes can be stored under a different name and collide with another entry.

**Fix.** Reject names whose byte length exceeds `NAME_MAX` before inserting them; never rely on `CStr256` to clamp user input.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 842

> ```diff
>     fn unlink({prompt}self, name: {prompt}str) -> Result<()> {
>         if is_dot_or_dotdot(name) {
> ```

Missing deletion authorization (major): `unlink()` removes an entry without checking write and search permission on the containing directory. A process that can resolve the parent can delete files from a write-protected directory.

**Fix.** Authorize deletion against the containing directory before removing the entry, including sticky-directory ownership rules where applicable.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 874

> ```diff
>     fn rmdir({prompt}self, name: {prompt}str) -> Result<()> {
>         if is_dot(name) {
> ```

Missing deletion authorization (major): `rmdir()` removes directories without checking write and search permission on the containing directory, allowing unauthorized directory removal.

**Fix.** Check containing-directory mutation permission and the directory ownership rules before removing the target.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 922

> ```diff
>     fn rename({prompt}self, old_name: {prompt}str, target: {prompt}Arc<dyn Inode>, new_name: {prompt}str) -> Result<()> {
>         if is_dot_or_dotdot(old_name) {
> ```

Missing rename authorization (major): `rename()` performs no authorization on either parent directory. A caller with search access can move or replace entries in directories where it lacks write permission.

**Fix.** Require write and search permission on both source and destination parents, and enforce sticky-directory restrictions before mutating either directory.

### `kernel/src/fs/ramfs/fs.rs` line 922

> ```diff
>     fn rename({prompt}self, old_name: {prompt}str, target: {prompt}Arc<dyn Inode>, new_name: {prompt}str) -> Result<()> {
>         if is_dot_or_dotdot(old_name) {
> ```

Silent filename truncation (major): `rename()` does not validate `new_name` against `NAME_MAX`, but later stores it through `CStr256::from(...)` or `append_entry(...)`. A long `renameat()` name can disagree with the dentry cache and create colliding or unreachable entries.

**Fix.** Validate both `old_name` and `new_name` by byte length before taking directory locks or mutating the namespace.
